# COMPREHENSIVE REVIEW — ROUND 2 (2026-07-15/16)
## The second full grade-check: the COMPLETED engine as code, as simulation, as product, against its ambition
### Fable 5 (surveyor/architect) synthesizing 23 survey agents · 8.7M survey tokens · 1,915 tool uses · ~64 min
### Baseline: branch `review-fixes-2026-07-08` @ 91a0c409 (post W-MOMENTUM "THE ENGINE IS COMPLETE" + W-COMPOSER-2 lift + window close). Read-only survey; no gate run (the master merge held the machine).
### COVERAGE: all 22 subsystem slices READ IN FULL (the entire ~490k-LOC tree) + the determinism dimension. 10 cross-cutting dimension reviewers died on the session limit — re-dispatch pending (§8). Raw structured results banked at `docs/review-r2/RAW_SURVEY_RESULTS.json` @ 3cc2dfbe.

---

## 1. VERDICT

**Zero critical findings across the entire tree — for the second consecutive round — and zero
constitutional violations anywhere.** 191 findings: 0 critical / 14 high / 68 medium / 109 low,
169 of them confirmed by code-path trace. Every slice graded A-range except two B+
(components-shell-commerce, data-tables). Five slices earned a flat A (spatial-engine,
lib-infra-copy, backend-functions, scripts-build-ci, tests-estate) — the test estate was called
"the strongest artifact in the repository," and the determinism reviewer called the randomness
architecture "structurally impossible to silently diverge."

The engine the fifteen post-close waves built is real, disciplined, and composed — but the
survey's central result is a *shape*, not a number: **the defect mass has migrated from inside
mechanisms to the seams between them.** Round 1 found broken mechanisms (proposals that never
applied, retreats that never lived). Round 2 finds almost none of that; instead it finds
mechanisms that are correct in isolation and incoherent in composition — a naval ledger that
never retires its records, movers that do not know a settlement died, two waves that clobber
each other's same-tick writes, a designed occupation transition that is unreachable because its
input records are deleted one line earlier. The second shape is **built-but-unwired**: at least
eight design deliverables (the generosity triage, the forecast's marginal attribution, the
politics receipts, the momentum entry-only law, the derived-config guard, the mechanism
whispers, the PDF arrival scene, `causalBandWord`) are implemented, tested, and consumed by
nothing. The waves out-ran the wiring pass.

Both shapes are cheaper to fix than what round 1 faced. Nearly every high is a one-commit fix
inside an existing pattern; the fix program's real work is *closure* — wiring, retiring,
surfacing — not repair.

Pre-fix grades, this round: **as code A−, as simulation substance A−, as product surface B+,
process/discipline A**. The distance from here to the product's own bar is one fix program plus
one surfacing wave — and the soak.

## 2. AS CODE

**What is genuinely excellent.** The constitution is enforced by machinery, not convention, and
the machinery held under fifteen waves of pressure: fail-closed rngContext (ambient draws throw
everywhere), the eslint determinism fence backed by source-regex twins that survive a skipped
lint, virtual dormancy flags with fenced byte-identity goldens per kernel, the frozen spatial
digest now `Object.freeze`d and reference-shared (round-1's perf class closed the bold way), the
residue-strip registry that turned pause/dismiss byte-equivalence from prose into a contract,
and a test estate whose defining property is that the guards guard the guards (unconditional
VERIFY_DIST hard-fail, e2e anti-vacuity tested against forged runner JSON, a weekly mutation
sweep that plants regressions to prove the spine catches them). Round-1 remediation is
verifiably real: across ten slices, surveyors found fixes annotated with their finding ids
in-code and confirmed no regression — with exactly one exception (the G2 terrain-boost rename
created double-stacking rows, data-tables-2).

**The two code-health debts that matter.**
1. **Guard-lag.** The prevention machinery itself has no ratchet that keeps it pointed at moving
   targets. Six instances: the data-purity RNG fence still guards `src/generators/` after the
   seam moved to `src/kernel/` (and the mutation sweep certifies the stale fence weekly); the
   guidance walker's "mounted" check is file-existence, so registered-but-never-rendered
   whispers pass; the adoption-telemetry whitelist froze at rev 7 while ~7 mover waves merged
   behind it; the architecture cycle baseline is DFS-order-dependent; the dormancy oracle lives
   inside a test file imported by 21 suites; `check` still runs dist-reading contracts before
   `build`. Each is one commit; together they are a class.
2. **Single-source drift at display edges.** The repo's own P11 doctrine (the drawn line and the
   key can never disagree) is violated by its newest surfaces: the relationship palette exists
   in three copies (one already divergent), war/faith channels draw twice in two purples,
   `worldSnapshotPublic` hand-duplicates three sibling selectors (already forked on deity
   naming), two surfaces read prosperity as an object where the engine emits a string, and
   DailyLife recomputes the food deficit the A+ pdf.3 fix banished everywhere else.

The god-file question (pulseKernel 2,454 lines, comment-borne lane composition that has now
bitten twice via add-a-lane-update-three-lists) is real but bounded: the tick-core surveyor
recommends a composed lane registry, and that is the right post-merge bold move.

## 3. AS SIMULATION SUBSTANCE

**The engine finally runs up as well as down, and honestly.** Booms are named compositions of
real trade throughput; reconstruction debits real ally credit; satellites are conserved
population transfers; the calamity bucket is genuinely type-blind with the strike budget
preserved under exposure loading. The belief→decision→consequence chain is closed end-to-end
(rumors → banded beliefs → Blainey margins read in both directions → typed reasons → sue-for-
peace → treaties → strain → revanchism), and the momentum threshold law holds *mechanically* —
the politics surveyor verified every psychology factor is floored; there is no absorbing state
in the slice. War homeostasis is closed (feasibility → exhaustion scar → age drain → hard
ceiling), conservation is audited two-sided with war dead as the only sink, and force ≡ organic
is structurally enforced through shared kernel resolvers, not asserted.

**The incoherence that remains is concentrated and nameable — cross-wave contract enforcement:**
- **The dead are not dead** (high): "movers skip remnants" is enforced nowhere outside the two
  lifecycle modules — upswing can arm reconstruction on a corpse, the annual calamity draw can
  re-strike a ruin for a "0 dead" beat, flourishing could found an Academy on a grave.
- **The navy lobotomy** (high): naval transit records never retire — one arrived convoy locks a
  navy out of all future operations forever, and blockades have no organic end (peace lands, the
  strangulation persists and keeps feeding the peace reason that already fired).
- **Same-tick clobber in a constitutional kernel** (high): upswing's `freshSettlement` reads the
  pre-clone array its sibling kernel correctly avoids — cross-arc writes silently lost,
  completed reconstructions re-armable.
- **Advertised transitions that cannot fire**: convergence's occupation-on-overstay is designed,
  tuned, and pin-tested, but intervention records are deleted the tick their contest resolves;
  the one-army law is enforced in only one direction; a DM's casus decree survives exactly one
  tick; boom cooling is dead code (booms can only end in crisis); settlementPolitics' two "who
  does" inputs read fields no engine code writes (plus an operator-precedence bug), so the
  external-threat rally and alignment kinship never fire.
- **Conservation frays**: same-tick multi-receiver generosity overdraws the hard reserve floor
  (the designed triage is built and unconsumed); the upswing's repayment fold double-decays the
  obligations ledger; a dead parent strands its satellites outside Σ-conservation.

None of these threaten determinism — every one produces the same wrong bytes on every run. That
is exactly why they survived fifteen green gates: the constitution proves *reproducibility*, and
these are reproducible incoherences. The counterpart criterion held everywhere *except* the one
verb whose dial is a placeholder (FORCE_RECONSIDERATION refuses on every UI-staged order — the
momentum wave's counterpart is dead at the surface).

## 4. AS PRODUCT / EXPERIENCE

**The engine has outrun the eye — confirmed, and now enumerable.** The one-mover-one-read-model
contract (articulated by this codebase's own display layer) is breached by the five newest
ledgers: politics blocs and covert conspiracies, foreign interventions, the credibility stock,
momentum, and resource dynamics are all written by the engine and read by no display surface.
The politics receipts (formation, fracture, exposure — the design's §6 promise) are computed and
discarded by the kernel. The forecast's headline deliverable — marginal attribution, the
with-vs-without diff for a staged order — is built, tested, and rendered nowhere. The composer
wave is "about 90% delivered at the surface where the DM actually lives."

**The trust surfaces have the sharpest product bugs.** Two highs sit on the commerce funnel's
narrowest points: the primary AuthModal closes itself when the user presses Enter to submit (a
failed sign-in can never show its error — sibling modals carry the one-line guard it lacks), and
the wizard save flow never stamps save identity into the store, so the owner-ruled free-tier
ladder (save → $2.99 durable right) dead-ends while duplicate save clicks burn the three free
slots and the exit dialog claims an already-saved draft is unsaved. Two more highs drop DM
intent silently: a clock-bound queue refusal rides through `applyEvent` shaped like success (the
composer resets the form for an order that never queued — live for the entire duration of a
parked pause), and the docket's edit lane inverts a queued cult *removal* into an *imposition*
(editSeed is a hand-maintained reverse of buildEvent with zero test coverage). The map autosave
key diverged from its own chip's fingerprint, so drag-edits show "Unsaved changes" forever and
are discarded on campaign switch. And two analytics calls ship raw user data (a Supabase userId,
free-typed search text) past the privacy floor the analytics program built.

Where the surfaces ARE wired, the product is unusually legible — cause chains as sentences,
refusals that teach, empty states as invitations, receipts in house voice everywhere, and the
newest read-models (treaty documents, climb-down receipts, misjudgment news) are excellent. The
gap is coverage, not craft.

## 5. AGAINST THE AMBITION (provisional — the ambition-fit dimension re-dispatches at reset)

Synthesizing from the 22 subsystem reports: the owner's bar — "be good at everything, excel at
the most important" — is met in the engine room and not yet at the table. The simulation depth,
determinism guarantees, and money-path engineering exceed what shipping competitors carry; the
DM-facing surfacing of the newest third of the engine, and the last-mile trust bugs above, are
what a first-hour paying DM would actually hit. The honest distance to the idealized product is:
one seam-closure fix program (§7), one surfacing wave (the read-model siblings + receipt
routing), the master merge, and the soak. No architectural rework is required anywhere — the
first round's architecture held under 15 waves of load, which is the strongest possible evidence
for it.

## 8. ~~RE-DISPATCH QUEUE~~ → COMPLETE (2026-07-16 ~00:45): all 33/33 slices landed

The 10 dimensions returned (2.4M further tokens): **full-fleet census 269 findings —

## 8b. DIMENSIONS ADDENDUM — what the cross-cutting lenses add to §1–§5

The dimensions confirm the two headline shapes (cross-wave seams; built-but-unwired) and add
four sharper facts:

1. **THE UNLIT SWITCHBOARD (sim-cohesion-4 — the biggest product fact of the round).** Every
   one of the fifteen post-close engine waves ships behind a virtual flag that **no preset and
   no UI toggle sets** — momentum, navy, intervention, lifecycle, upswing, resource dynamics,
   peace engine, supply-web, constructive flows are all unreachable by any user path, and the
   composer's teaching refusals direct the DM to switches that do not render. Three gates have
   recorded owner-gated lighting; six have NO ruling on file. The anti-stasis engine is
   complete and structurally dark. (Preset lighting is owner-gated by standing law — this
   joins the §6 owner list as its most consequential member.)
2. **THE AUTHORITY GATE IS HOLLOW AT ITS CENTER (experience-product-fit-1, high).** The
   pause-for-your-verdict loop — the product's core authority promise — has engine support for
   per-major verdicts but **no surface renders the batched majors**; the only resume affordance
   silently resolves every major to defaults while the panel copy says "paused for your
   decisions."
3. **THE REGRESSED-CLASS PATTERN (content-immersion, 2 highs).** Round-1 fiction fixes (CI-1
   token leaks, CI-2 crier mis-routes) were correct but guarded by HAND-LISTS, not walkers —
   so five post-fix waves regressed the class: booms get market-shortage crier quotes; relief
   reads as "word of generosity relief" in player rumors. Same lesson as the guard-lag class:
   a fix without a walker re-opens one wave later.
4. **DEPLOY-PATH DOC ROT IS ON THE CRITICAL PATH (docs-knowledge-1, high).** DEPLOY.md — the
   first-cutover runbook for the now-pre-authorized merge→push→deploy — says "through
   migration 046" (actual: 134) and "10 functions total" (actual: 16, omitting auth-recovery
   and checkout verification). Must be fixed (with a freshness pin) BEFORE Phase O executes.

Also material: the security second wall (the world-snapshot hard-deny census) stopped growing
15 waves ago — no live leak (the allowlist holds), and the dedicated sibling-hunt found **no
third covert text lane** beyond the two recorded ones, which is genuine good news. The perf
lens found round-1's prescribed fix shipped only half (the ensured-brand fast-path exists,
adopted at one call site) and the forecast runs its 52-tick compute on the main thread when
the advance's worker transport already exists. Sim-cohesion names a new sub-shape — *taxonomies
wired at the exit but not the entry* (intervention momentum deposits, the conscience door, boom
receipt sources) — and one fresh owner-law violation: upswing's peace read is blockade-blind
(golden ages can mint under naval strangulation, against "a blockade is the same as a siege").
Ambition-fit's verdict: the tree EXCEEDS its own vision docs in six shipped strata the vision
never names, while the vision's single most quotable line (`warCausalBrief` — "N of M peace
reasons now present; this war is dying") is computed every tick and rendered by zero surfaces.

**§6 OWNER-URGENT — two additions:** (4) the wave-lighting ruling (which of the nine dark
gates light, in which preset — the M10b preset precedent; six gates have no ruling);
(5) DEPLOY.md refresh sign-off rides before any deploy (agent-fixable, flagged for timing).

**§7 amendments:** W-R2-SURFACE grows (pause-verdict cards, navalStrength/blockade standing
state, warCausalBrief mount, hegemony read, war-room treaty table, docket edit deep-link,
ctx-correct lapse predicates); W-R2-GUARDS grows (impactKind walker for WHAT_PHRASES/newsVoice,
size-ratchet frozen ceilings + store/pdf/lib coverage, transitive headless-spine walker,
seeded property tests, ensured-brand adoption, hard-deny derivation from CONDITIONAL_LEDGER_KEYS);
new **W-R2-DOCS** (DEPLOY/ARCHITECTURE/CONTRIBUTING freshness pins, playbook desk pointers,
round-21 W2 trap line, RISK_REGISTER demotion). The pulseKernel lane-registry extraction is
confirmed by two independent lenses (tick-core + code-quality) — it lands post-merge with
per-file frozen ceilings in the same commit.

### Slice: worldpulse-tick-core (grade A-)

**Coverage:** Read HEAD 91a0c409 (expected). Scope = 13 files / 11,740 LOC, ALL fully read: pulseKernel.js (2,454), applyWorldPulse.js (1,434), worldState.js (616), stressors.js (713), stressorDynamics.js (992), stressorGates.js (769), causeLifecycle.js (610), factionCompetition.js (736), institutionLifecycle.js (1,004), settlementStrategy.js (1,029), disposition.js (570), simulationSpine.js (278), timeProgression.js (535). Because the slice attention names interval orchestration/pause guards, I also fully read the adjacent advanceInterval.js (454), candidateEvents.js (462), pulseHelpers.js (156), residueStripGuard.js (122), and decisionTier.js (~120), plus targeted reads of relationshipState.js, region/graph.js (edge-id normalization), actorMajorApproval.js (expiry guard), npcAgency.relaxNpcStates, rulingPower.js banding, and servicesGenerator/economyReconcilePass (schema shape checks). Skipped: the individual mover kernels (supply/naval/upswing/etc. — owned by sibling slices), warDeployment.js internals, and tests (read-only discipline; no gates run). Deferral ledgers checked: round-1 register §worldpulse-core (11 findings + verdicts), COMPREHENSIVE_REVIEW_PROGRAM.md fix tracks, RESIDUE_STRIP_SITES in-file registry, in-file KNOWN-DEFERRED notes ([worldpulse-core-3]/[worldpulse-core-4]/[worldpulse-core-6] political-autonomy ruling).

- **[MEDIUM|confirmed] worldpulse-tick-core-1** — Dismissed majors still drive moral drift, misjudgment news, and the tempo fold — consequence readers iterate selectedForApply, bypassing the dismissal filter (`src/domain/worldPulse/pulseKernel.js:1922`)
  - Evidence: Line 1922: `for (const outcome of selectedForApply) { const mis = outcome?.metadata?.misjudgment; …` — selectedForApply (line 1274) is never filtered by activeDismissals; only outcomesToApply (1289-1293) is. Also line 1690 `beliefMisjudgmentNewsEntries(selectedForApply, …)` and line 1321 `foldNarrativeTempo(…, selectedForApply, …)`. deriveDecisionTier confirms strategy_deploy is a dismissible major.
  - Why: On a resume where the DM dismissed a strategy_deploy carrying metadata.misjudgment, the dismissed march still permanently drifts the actor's alignment (spatialLedgers.moralDrift write + possible reckoning news), emits a wizardNews receipt asserting the march happened, and counts as a landed birth in the tempo ledger — effects of an action the DM vetoed. The conquest/deploy residue strips scrub exactly this class from the war ledgers; these three consumers bypass the discipline.
  - Fix shape: Feed the consequence readers the dismissal-filtered set (selectedForApply minus activeDismissals — the same filter outcomesToApply uses), keeping pulseRecord's deliberate full-set recording untouched. Add the moralDrift/tempo stores to the residue-guard checkers or its documented allowlist.
  - Deferral check: RESIDUE_STRIP_SITES registry (covers 4 war stores only; guard self-describes as a lower bound), round-1 register worldpulse-core + war-2..9 fixes, in-file pulseRecord full-set comment (scoped to pulseHistory byte-identity, not these readers). No recorded decision covers this.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[MEDIUM|plausible] worldpulse-tick-core-2** — Pending proposals are silently evicted by the 80-record ring under dm_only/recommendations during long advances (`src/domain/worldPulse/worldState.js:596`)
  - Evidence: `proposals: [...byId.values()].slice(-MAX_PROPOSALS)` (upsertProposal; MAX_PROPOSALS=80, line 10) — no status-aware pruning, no expiry receipt. rollCandidates admits up to 5 proposals/tick; candidateEvents' autonomy routing forces EVERY stochastic family to 'proposal' under dm_only/recommendations.
  - Why: A one_year advance (52 weekly ticks) under the forcing modes can mint ~260 proposals; the ring keeps the last 80, silently dropping pending decisions the DM never saw — including possibly still-pending ones — with no 'expired' stamp. The actor-major lane expires visibly (expire-to-decline); ordinary proposals just vanish. This undermines the exact DM-authority contract the forcing modes exist to provide.
  - Fix shape: Prune resolved (applied/dismissed/expired/refused) records first; give pending overflow a visible expire-to-decline stamp mirroring expireStaleActorMajors; or raise/partition the cap for forcing modes. Bold: one proposal-docket module owning retention policy with receipts.
  - Deferral check: Round-1 register (no proposal-cap finding), PHASE55 playbook §0.0.2, in-file comments (cap comment only addresses pathological queueing of pendingEvents, not proposals).
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] worldpulse-tick-core-3** — Resume recomputes intervalStartTick from the cursor's pre-tick world, weakening the in-advance expiry protection for actor-majors minted before the pause (`src/domain/worldPulse/advanceInterval.js:335`)
  - Evidence: `const intervalStartTick = ensureWorldState(runningCampaign?.worldState…).tick;` runs AFTER the resume block re-seats runningCampaign.worldState = resume.preWorldState — so on resume it is the paused tick's world tick, not the original advance start. expireStaleActorMajors protects only `Number(p.tick) < startTick` mints (actorMajorApproval.js:133).
  - Why: An actor-major (held war declaration/coup) minted during the first segment of a paused advance loses its minted-during-this-advance protection on resume: p.tick < resumeStartTick, so it expires-to-decline once 6 weeks pass inside the remaining segment — before the DM's post-advance panel. Partially re-opens the [worldpulse-core-3] fix on multi-segment advances.
  - Fix shape: Park the ORIGINAL intervalStartTick on the pause cursor (beside preIntervalHistoryLen) and thread it on resume; fall back to the recomputed value for legacy cursors.
  - Deferral check: Round-1 [worldpulse-core-3] (fixed in Track G — this is a gap in that fix's resume leg, not a re-find); advanceInterval cursor-shape comments record preIntervalHistoryLen compatibility but nothing for intervalStartTick.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] worldpulse-tick-core-4** — pulseRecord.candidateCount omits the lifecycle and moral-founding lanes; the returned candidates array omits moral-founding — round-1 worldpulse-core-7 persists and the new W-LIFECYCLE lane repeated it (`src/domain/worldPulse/pulseKernel.js:1583`)
  - Evidence: Line 1583 sums candidates+tierResource+resourceDyn+instLifecycle+moralInst+structural+coup+war — missing lifecycleCand.candidates.length and moralFounding.candidates.length, though both ride stochasticCandidates (line 1246). Line 2430's returned `candidates` includes lifecycleCand but still omits moralFounding.
  - Why: The persisted pulse receipt (and the composed interval's candidates surface) under-reports what the engine actually rolled — receipt honesty for the DM and for balance probes. The same omission was found in round 1 (worldpulse-core-7, low/confirmed, not in fix tracks) and the newly-landed lifecycle lane repeated half of it, proving the add-a-lane-update-three-lists pattern is a live bug habitat.
  - Fix shape: Compose ONE `stochasticLanes` array used for stochasticCandidates, candidateCount, and the return surface (structural prevention: new lanes join once). Byte-caution: candidateCount rides persisted pulseHistory — count changes shift record bytes on affected ticks.
  - Deferral check: Round-1 register [worldpulse-core-7] (recorded, low, absent from F/G fix tracks — persisting, not regressed); the lifecycleCand half is NEW (wave landed post-round-1).
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] worldpulse-tick-core-5** — Legitimacy band table duplicated verbatim in timeProgression.reBand and rulingPower.js — no shared source, no cross-pin (`src/domain/timeProgression.js:149`)
  - Evidence: reBand (lines 149-181) hard-codes `>=75 'Endorsed' #1a5a28 gov 1.30 crim 0.75 … <30 'Legitimacy Crisis'` plus the is* flags — byte-for-byte the same table as rulingPower.js:221-236. Grep confirms exactly these two copies.
  - Why: Every weekly tick re-bands publicLegitimacy through timeProgression's copy while coups/generation band through rulingPower's; a future tuning of thresholds/multipliers in one file silently forks the settlement's legitimacy semantics between the tick path and the transfer path — a classic drift-prone duplicate on a load-bearing scalar.
  - Fix shape: Export one band function from rulingPower.js (or a shared leaf, minding first-paint weight) and import it in timeProgression; byte-identical today, so no golden shift.
  - Deferral check: Round-1 register (grep reBand/timeProgression: nothing), DEAD_CODE_DISPOSITION.md, playbook deferral ledger — no record.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|plausible] worldpulse-tick-core-6** — causeLifecycle exposure terminal misses bearers already revealed at attribution, and the revealed-institution check applies settlement-wide against its own documented scope (`src/domain/worldPulse/causeLifecycle.js:345`)
  - Evidence: Terminal 1 requires a crossing: `situation === 'compromised-revealed' && record.situation !== 'compromised-revealed'`. A bearer attributed while ALREADY revealed (timesExposed>0, corruptionImpaired, or ctx.revealedInstitutions>0) starts at situation 'compromised-revealed', never crosses, and proceeds through the QUIET covert lifecycle. bearerRevealed's `ctx.revealedInstitutions > 0` applies to every bearer despite the comment scoping it to corruption "homed in the watch/court".
  - Why: The module's own golden law says once public, quiet evolution stops and the scandal machinery owns the arc — yet a pre-W-C5 save's already-impaired corrupt NPC (or any bearer in a town with one revealed security institution) quietly re-causes/reforms/historicizes, producing covert-voiced receipts for a public scandal. The settlement-wide revealed flip also stamps every bearer's conjunction-key situation at once, coarsening the W2 content seam.
  - Fix shape: At attribution, stamp stage 'exposed-public' when the initial situation is already revealed; scope the institution-bucket reveal to bearers whose leash/home resolves to the revealed institution.
  - Deferral check: W-C5 memory + in-file design header (documents the crossing law itself — the born-revealed edge is unrecorded); no register/ledger entry.

- **[LOW|confirmed] worldpulse-tick-core-7** — Inert strategy postures (hold/defend) publish as 'major' significance feed entries — MOVE_SEVERITY 0.72 exactly meets the feed's major threshold (`src/domain/worldPulse/applyWorldPulse.js:282`)
  - Evidence: `let major = outcome.applyMode === 'proposal' || outcome.severity >= 0.72 || …` — every strategy candidate is emitted at MOVE_SEVERITY = 0.72 (settlementStrategy.js:118), including the deliberately inert 'defend'/'hold' markers, whose candidateType isn't npc_* so the significance demotion never applies. Score ≈ 58+18 = 76.
  - Why: With the strategy layer lit, each settlement's status-quo posture headlines as MAJOR chronicle news ("X holds its posture", score 76) — outranking genuine notables; metronome suppression only mutes repeats within 6 ticks, so it re-emits indefinitely. The severity floor exists for exclusive-group ranking, not narrative weight — the two concerns are conflated at this seam.
  - Fix shape: Demote inert posture markers (strategy_hold/strategy_defend, and lever candidates without a nudge) below the major cut in newsEntryForOutcome — mirror the npc_* significance-honesty carve-out — or suppress their feed entry entirely (the ledger keeps them).

The war-2 fix made this ordering load-bearing.

(b) relaxFactionStates/relaxNpcStates multiply momentum/heat by 0.85-0.9 forever (no epsilon floor; values persist as 1e-30 dust). (c) applyWorldPulse.js:1379/1393 type news entries and campaign as `SimSettlement['config']`.

(b) is serialization hygiene — decayed-but-never-zero scalars churn save bytes and diff noise for centuries of ticks. (c) defeats the checker as thoroughly as `any` while claiming zero any-holes — misleading for the next maintainer.

- **[HIGH|confirmed] worldpulse-war-military-1** — navalTransit records never retire: arrived convoys and orphaned records persist forever, permanently locking a navy out of future operations (`src/domain/worldPulse/navalKernel.js:236`)
  - Evidence: "for (const key of Object.keys(prior).sort()) { ... records[key] = stepArmyPosition(rec, nowTick); }" — all prior records carry forward; the only removal is sea-battle loss (delete records[loserKey]). Contrast armyTransitKernel.js:211-240, which re-derives records from live deployments each tick (self-pruning). No caller prunes on hasArrived, deployment resolution, or canon membership (grep: navalTransit consumers are supplyKernel guard, momentum reads, realmVerbExecution mints/one delete).
  - Why: The owner's most-bitten class: a convoy whose siege resolved (or that simply arrived, position01=1) persists forever; both convoy-derive and blockade-derive skip any navy with a record ('the fleet is already at sea'), so one convoy is a one-shot lobotomy for that navy; the ledger never drops (dormancy drop-when-empty unreachable); records for removed settlements also persist.
  - Fix shape: Mirror the land kernel: prune any naval record whose escorted deployment is gone/recalled-and-home, whose convoy hasArrived plus a return leg (or immediate stand-down), or whose owner/target left snapshot.byId. Add a pin: convoy arrives → record retires within N ticks; navy can re-operate.
  - Deferral check: Not in playbook §0.0.2/§0.6; DESIGN_NAVY §7 pins list no convoy-retirement pin; W-NAVY postdates the round-1 register; no in-file deferral note covers record retirement.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[MEDIUM|confirmed] worldpulse-war-military-2** — One-army law enforced in only one direction: an intervening settlement can still open a siege (deployments gate never consults the interventions ledger) (`src/domain/worldPulse/warDeployment.js:1651`)
  - Evidence: Step 4: "if (deployments[fromId]) continue; // one-army constraint" — no read of the interventions spatial ledger (grep confirms zero 'interventions' references in warDeployment.js). convergence.js:967-970 enforces only the reverse ("busy = new Set(Object.keys(deployments))").
  - Why: convergence.js:47-49 claims "a settlement cannot besiege AND intervene" and DESIGN_CONVERGENCE.md:108-109 states the one-army law as bidirectional physics; in the lit world a settlement that committed an intervention column last tick passes every deploy gate and fields a second army, breaking the stated invariant and double-counting its strength.
  - Fix shape: In warDeployment step 4, skip fromId when interventionActive(worldState) and the interventions ledger holds any record with interId === fromId (a cheap lazy read; 0 when dark ⇒ byte-identical). Pin: intervening settlement blocked from opening a siege until its column returns.
  - Deferral check: The in-file JUDGMENT (convergence.js:42-50) records the isolated-ledger choice and the forward direction only; playbook W-CONVERGENCE deferral list (verbs, INTERCEPT seam, rebellion tilts) does not cover the reverse gate.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[MEDIUM|confirmed] worldpulse-war-military-3** — Occupation-on-overstay is orphaned: designed, tuned, and pin-tested but unreachable — intervention records are deleted the same tick their contest resolves (`src/domain/worldPulse/convergence.js:990`)
  - Evidence: "if (liveTargets.has(rec.target)) continue; // still contested — no aftermath yet / delete next[k];" — records exit immediately on resolution; no resolvedTick is ever stamped and overstayOccupation (line 685, OVERSTAY_TICKS=4) has zero src callers outside convergence.js (grep). RETREATED/BESIEGING/SCREENING states are also never assigned (only HOLDING/ATTRITED are written).
  - Why: DESIGN_CONVERGENCE.md:36 lists "on overstay after victory — the OCCUPATION transition" as a wave feature and the pin table (line 158) names it; a victorious intervener converting to occupier is the design's bridge into the occupation layer, and it can never fire — an advertised mechanism that is dead code in the live path.
  - Fix shape: Stamp resolvedTick on records whose contest resolved instead of deleting; dwell them, call overstayOccupation each tick, feed its conquest-shaped outcome into freshConquestsFrom, and delete on occupy-or-depart. Alternatively record it as an explicit deferral row — today it is neither wired nor recorded.

- **[MEDIUM|plausible] worldpulse-war-military-4** — A blockade has no organic end: peace or hostility-normalization never lifts it, leaving a standing strangulation that contradicts the settled war (`src/domain/worldPulse/navalKernel.js:271`)
  - Evidence: hostileOwners gates only NEW mints (pickBlockadeTarget); no leg removes a BLOCKADE record when the owner–target relationship stops being hostile. Removal paths found: sea-battle loss (navalKernel.js:379) and the DM's FORCE_RECONSIDERATION momentum crack (realmVerbExecution.js:410-417). blockadeStrangulationOf keeps feeding the economic_strangulation peace reason meanwhile.
  - Why: Blockade-is-a-siege is the owner's law, and sieges have three closers (withdrawal, ceiling, recall); the blockade has none that fires organically. Perverse loop: strangulation drives the target to sue for peace, peace lands, the blockade persists and keeps strangling — simulation incoherence on the sea half of the parity law.
  - Fix shape: Add an organic lift: in advanceNaval, retire a BLOCKADE record whose owner–target pair is no longer hostile (or whose momentum course cracked), with a lift news beat; mirror the land layer's stale-front retirement. Pin: peace label lands → blockade lifts within one tick.

- **[MEDIUM|plausible] worldpulse-war-military-5** — DECLARE_CASUS decree survives exactly one tick: the reason fold recomputes every pair from state-derived scorers and silently drops any decreed record they do not back (`src/domain/worldPulse/warReasons.js:554`)

A decree minted by declareCasus (line 633) at severity 0.6 is overwritten/dropped at the next tick's fold unless live state independently produces the same type ≥ MIN_SCORE.
  - Why: The verb reads as a lasting decree ("mint (or renew)"; force ≡ organic) and feeds the deploy-gate casus lift, but it influences at most one evaluateWarLayer pass (mover order: war layer at pulseKernel:785 reads it, advanceWarReasons at :2323 erases it). A DM who declares a casus and ticks twice sees their decree vanish with no receipt — surprising, unrecorded semantics on a paid-surface verb.
  - Fix shape: Carry decreed records through the fold with explicit decay (e.g. a decreedUntilTick or a per-tick score ramp-down over ~8 ticks, max-merged with the state-derived score), or document one-tick semantics in the verb's manifest prose and glossary. Pin either way.
  - Deferral check: In-file W-PEACE-1 deferral notes cover news-emission and treaty/corruption seams only; W-COMPOSER-2 rows record the registration, not decree persistence.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_

(1-Number(st.malice01)) : NaN`. - **[HIGH|confirmed] worldpulse-economy-upswing-1** — "Movers skip remnants" is enforced nowhere outside the lifecycle modules — upswing, calamity, resource dynamics, and generosity all treat a terminal-dead settlement as live (`src/domain/worldPulse/upswingKernel.js:401`)

DESIGN_SETTLEMENT_LIFECYCLE §1: a remnant's cell survives and "movers skip it".
  - Why: The terminal-death writer leaves prosperity/legitimacy/calamityHistory intact, so a reconstruction can arm on the corpse (the killing calamity is a within-window trigger), the annual draw can re-strike a ruin (a "0 dead" news beat), and flourishing could found an Academy on a remnant — visible simulation incoherence violating the wave's own constitution.
  - Fix shape: One shared remnant predicate (lifecycleStatusOf, exported from settlementLifecycleFirstClass) consumed at every mover's settlement enumeration (upswing B1/B2/B3, calamity annual loop, resource-dynamics loop, generosity considerOrientation), plus a movers-skip-remnants pin over a die-then-advance fixture.
  - Deferral check: DESIGN_SETTLEMENT_LIFECYCLE (V2 graduation is the only recorded seam; mover-skip is stated as design law, not deferred); playbook §0.0.1 W-LIFECYCLE row; memory w-upswing/w-discovery deferral lists — none record this.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_

Two GIVE verdicts to one giver both price the full headroom. grep: triageAllocation has zero production consumers (tests only).

  - Fix shape: Track a per-giver remaining-headroom map inside the ask loop (decrement spareableMonths by each lostMonths before the next ask prices it), or wire triageAllocation over same-tick claimants per giver; add a two-claimant floor pin.

- **[MEDIUM|confirmed] worldpulse-economy-upswing-3** — Boom hysteresis exit is unimplemented — BOOM_EXIT_THROUGHPUT is consumed only pre-mint, so a boom never cools and can band-step prosperity up forever (`src/domain/worldPulse/upswingKernel.js:610`)
  - Evidence: Tuning :111 documents BOOM_EXIT_THROUGHPUT 1.5 as "hysteresis: below this a boom cools (not yet bust)", but grep shows its only consumer is the 'building' branch (:628). The boom-sustain branch checks only severed = embattled || throughput < BUST_THROUGHPUT(1.0) || veinGone.
  - Why: A boom whose throughput settles into [1.0, 1.5) sustains indefinitely, drifting prosperity up a band every ~7 ticks until the ladder cap — the documented graceful-cooling path is dead, so booms can only end in a bust condition (a crisis) even when trade merely normalizes.
  - Fix shape: Add a cooling branch to the boom phase: throughput < BOOM_EXIT_THROUGHPUT (and not severed) ⇒ drop the boom record + clear the boom condition without the bust retreat/legitimacy hit; receipt it as boom_cooled.
  - Deferral check: DESIGN_UPSWING §2 B2 + §5 deferrals (hysteresis is specced, cooling not deferred); playbook W-UPSWING ledger row and memory w-upswing-shipped deferrals — not recorded.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_

  - Why: On every tick a reconstruction consumes ally credit, all obligations realm-wide decay twice — an unreceipted conservation drift that erodes debts faster than the tuned rate, weakening the "aid changes history" ledger the §7 soak reads.

- **[MEDIUM|confirmed] worldpulse-economy-upswing-5** — Terminal death of a parent with live satellite steadings strands them frozen in the ledger, outside the conservation story (`src/domain/worldPulse/settlementLifecycleKernel.js:547`)
  - Evidence: if (parent0.lifecycleStatus || parent0.config?.lifecycleStatus) continue; // remnants seed nothing — the continue skips B2 (grow/starve/return), B3, B4, and B5 entirely, so a dead parent's steadings never starve, never return residents, and persist in spatialLedgers.satellites indefinitely.
  - Why: A demoted town→thorp parent can still hold steadings when it terminally dies; their population (debited from the parent in life) dangles outside Σ(parent+satellites) forever, and a later resettlement inherits the dead town's orbit — the design's "conservation at every edge" has an unhandled edge at the grave.
  - Fix shape: On terminal death (or in the satellite lane when the parent reads as a remnant): run the abandonment path for each steading — residual population disperses through the same migration ledger as the parent's, records removed, receipted; pin over a die-with-steadings fixture.
  - Deferral check: DESIGN_SETTLEMENT_LIFECYCLE §1/§4 (conservation pin claims Σ across ANY lifecycle event; V2 graduation is the only recorded seam); playbook W-LIFECYCLE row — not recorded.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_

  - Fix shape: Mint a distinct 'calamity_no_target' code + prose ('no such member settlement — the target left the campaign'); add it to coversVetoCodes so the walker's prose check covers it.
  - Deferral check: No in-file note; realm walker's prose test passes because the code renders SOME prose — just the wrong one.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] composer-realm-verbs-7** — FORCE_ABANDON's target options list ALL living settlements while only thorp-tier passes the kernel, so the mint queues knowably-doomed orders despite LAW 1's own claim (`src/domain/events/realmManifest.js:394`)
  - Evidence: targetOptions: (ws, ctx) => lifecycleTargets(ctx, false) — every living member; forceAbandonSettlement (settlementLifecycleFirstClass.js) refuses `if (tier !== 'thorp')`. mintRealmVerbProposal's comment: 'the composer never queues a knowably-doomed order' — but the verb-level predicate is not target-aware.
  - Why: Design §3: options are CURRENT-STATE-FILTERED ('the target dial for EXPOSE_CORRUPTION lists only actually-compromised entities'). A DM staging abandonment of a city gets a pending proposal that can only ever refuse — visible, but a preview-honesty dilution on a campaign-altering verb.
  - Fix shape: Filter FORCE_ABANDON's targetOptions to living thorp-tier members (same-function read: popToTier/tier — the kernel's own gate), keeping the 'demote it first' unlock hint on the predicate for the empty case.
  - Deferral check: No in-file note; the entry's unlock prose acknowledges the thorp rule, but the option filter contradicts it; not in any ledger.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] composer-realm-verbs-8** — Every 'target' dial shares the verb-level targetOptions, so multi-target verbs show the wrong primary option set (ORDER_INTERVENTION's patron dial lists contested settlements first) (`src/components/map/RealmVerbComposer.jsx:131`)
  - Evidence: {(active.entry.targetOptions?.(worldState, ctx) || memberOptions).map(...)} — the SAME options render for patronId and targetId; the appended 'Members always listable' fallback (line 136) papers over it.

  - Fix shape: Resolve options per dial: give each target dial an optionsFrom key ('campaignSettlements' | 'coupContests' | 'ports' | 'belligerents' | 'courses') the manifest maps to its shared reader, and have the UI call the dial-level resolver.

- **[LOW|confirmed] composer-realm-verbs-9** — The forecast pane reports refusals as a bare count — the lapsed entries are never named, unlike the real advance's per-settlement refusal news (`src/components/map/RealmForecast.jsx:84`)
  - Evidence: {view.refusals.length} queued orders would be REFUSED at the tick (lapsed against the current world). — run.refusals carry queueId/saveId/eventType/code/detail, all dropped.
  - Why: The forecast exists so the DM can fix the docket BEFORE the tick; a count without names sends them hunting. The real drain names each refusal per settlement (queueRefusalNews) — the preview should match the apply's legibility.
  - Fix shape: Render each refusal with settlement name + event type + realmVetoProse-style reason (the data is already in run.refusals); link to the docket entry.

- **[LOW|confirmed] composer-realm-verbs-10** — Recorded deferral (reporting per instructions): party-caused queued events' world-side ripple is absent from the forecast clone — preview≡apply binds only on non-party queues (`src/domain/worldPulse/forecastRun.js:20`)
  - Evidence: KNOWN DIVERGENCE (documented, not a gap to re-find): party-caused queued events replay their world-side party ripple through the STORE (recordPartyImpact) after the real drain; the forecast applies their settlement-side effect but not that ripple.
  - Why: A docket containing party-caused events forecasts a slightly smaller future than the advance delivers (missing faction/NPC world-state + condition ripple). Documented honestly in-file with the fix seam named.
  - Fix shape: As the in-file note prescribes: a party-impact domain seam (extract buildPartyImpactOutcomes application the way applyTwinDirectivesToWorld was extracted) so the clone-run replays it; or surface the caveat in the pane when the queue contains party-caused entries.
  - Deferral check: In-file KNOWN DIVERGENCE block at forecastRun.js:20-25 — a recorded deferral; reported at low per the survey protocol.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] composer-realm-verbs-11** — Parked-factory spread leaves stale entry fields on realm manifest entries: FORCE_CALAMITY/FORCE_FOUND_STEADING/FORCE_ABANDON/FORCE_RESETTLE carry scope:'settlement' where the manifest contract declares scope:'realm' (`src/domain/events/realmManifest.js:361`)
  - Evidence: FORCE_CALAMITY: Object.freeze({ ...calamityParked, verb, label, lane, ... }) — calamityParked has scope:'settlement' (calamityKernel.js forceCalamityEntry) and no override follows; the manifest's own entry-shape comment (line 166-170) says scope:'realm'.
  - Why: No consumer reads .scope on realm entries today (UI and walker don't), so impact is zero now — but the realm walker doesn't pin scope, so the first consumer that filters by scope ('realm' vs 'settlement' verbs) will silently drop the four force verbs. Contract drift of the class spread-order bugs breed.
  - Fix shape: Add scope:'realm' to the four spread entries (or a scope override in the shared spread tail) and one walker assertion `expect(v.scope).toBe('realm')` — mirrors the settlement walker's scope pin.

- **[MEDIUM|confirmed] spatial-engine-1** — Banditry danger keys on the ORIGIN's embattlement — the one node the route-danger model deliberately excludes — so a caravan through a war zone from a calm producer suffers zero banditry (`src/domain/spatial/supplyShipments.js:367`)
  - Evidence: `const danger = embattlementLevel(ctx.worldState, record.sourceId);` (M2 arrival) and identically commodityFlow.js:640; meanwhile scoreRoute sums danger over `for (let i = 1; i < nodes.length; i++)` — intermediaries+destination, origin EXCLUDED (embattlement.js:353). Design §6: an embattled region ON A ROUTE 'sporadically drops a shipment to banditry'.
  - Why: Sim incoherence at the flagship trade seam: route CHOICE prices embattled ground correctly, but the realized loss reads the single node routing ignores. A besieged destination or war-zone intermediary inflicts no banditry; a calm road from an embattled producer does. The two danger models contradict.
  - Fix shape: Both arrival sites already call chooseRoute — use the returned ScoredRoute.danger (per-hop mean or max) as banditryLoss's dangerLevel instead of source-node embattlement. Behavior-shifting: the supplyShipments.test.js:289 'embattled source' pin and any lit-world goldens move — declare the one-time shift.
  - Deferral check: No row in playbook §0.0.2/§0.6/§0.8, round-1 register (spatial-engine-9 nit batch + misc-2 checked), or in-file seam notes; the in-file headers claim 'shipments through embattled ground ride banditryLoss', contradicting the code.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[MEDIUM|confirmed] spatial-engine-2** — commodityFlowEnabled mid-campaign flip has no representation-reconciliation: carried goods silently vanish (ON→OFF), and a months-stale commodityStocks snapshot resumes as live truth (OFF→ON) (`src/domain/spatial/commodityFlow.js:565`)
  - Evidence: M6a ARRIVE drops M2-shaped records: `if (!rec.sourceId || rec.carried <= 0) continue;` — an OFF→ON flip evaporates every in-transit M2 caravan. ON→OFF: M2's stepSupplyLink rehydrates M6a records but its ShipmentRecord shape has no `carried` — quantities vanish unbooked; commodityStocks persists (dormancy law) and the cold-start guard (line 500) only fills ABSENT keys, so re-enable resumes frozen stale stocks verbatim.
  - Why: The two representations share the supplyShipments ledger key and are explicitly 'one or the other, never both' — but the flip is a supported surface (full_simulation preset lights the flag, simulationRules.js:300; rules changes are receipted mid-campaign like infoMode). This is exactly the owner's most-bitten class: state that survives one lifecycle path and ghosts another. Goods-conservation is only asserted intra-tick, so the break is invisible.
  - Fix shape: A representation-transition step at the rules-change chokepoint: on ON→OFF convert carried quantities into buffer-weeks (or book them lost with a receipt) and drop/mark commodityStocks; on OFF→ON re-cold-start stocks and translate M2 records. Alternatively record the flip as one-way (a preset downgrade refuses or warns) — either way, write the decision down.
  - Deferral check: Playbook §0.6 rows 50-51 record only the preset LIGHTING; no transition-policy row anywhere. commodityFlow header documents gating ('never both') but not the mid-campaign flip. Round-1 register has no such finding.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[MEDIUM|plausible] spatial-engine-3** — Re-canonize swaps the geometry but carries every in-transit spatialLedger forward verbatim — armies and convoys keep marching persisted old-canon paths (`src/store/campaignSpatialCanonize.js:106`)
  - Evidence: `c.worldState = { ...ws, spatialCanonVersion: nextVersion, spatialDigest: digest };` — spatialLedgers untouched. armyTransit/navalTransit records PERSIST `path` + `arrivalTick` priced under the old canon; currentRegion/remainingRegions/detectCollisions/seaEdgesOfPath (navalLayer.js:287) all read record.path against the NEW digest.
  - Why: Re-canonize is the designed mid-campaign event ('ports EMERGE the moment a settlement founds a harbour'; teleport 'a new circle changes destiny') and is DM-reachable via LivingWorldGates. Caravans self-heal (routes recomputed per tick) but an army's stale path can traverse pairs no longer adjacent, and a convoy mid-sea whose lane was pruned reads zero sea edges — silent geometry-truth divergence. Design §VI open-question 3 covers cost-law policy for CANONS, not in-transit reconciliation.
  - Fix shape: A small reconcile pass in canonize: re-plan (or force-arrive/retreat) any transit record whose path contains a pair not adjacent in the new digest, and re-price arrivalTicks; stamp a receipt. Or guard: refuse re-canonize while columns are afield (deferral-visible, the convoy-capacity idiom).
  - Deferral check: PHASE55_SPATIAL_ENGINE_DESIGN §VI.4 open-question 3 (cost-law re-canonize policy) checked — it addresses installed-base canons, not in-transit ledgers; no §0.0.2/§0.6 row; no in-file seam note in canonize or armyTransit.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] spatial-engine-4** — Nit batch: receipt honesty + doc-vs-code slips, one line each (`src/domain/spatial/generosityEV.js:770`)
  - Evidence: (a) generosityEV.js:770 GIVE_PARTIAL receipt hardcodes 'A tenth of what was asked' while magnitudeFraction can be up to 0.84 — under the engine's own 'the terms ARE the receipt' §H law, the receipt asserts a false quantity. (b) moralDrift.js:38 header calls itself 'a worldPulse leaf' — it is a spatial leaf. (c) cultureDistance.js:46 claims 'two identical settlements read EXACTLY 0', but two vectors with empty governingName read identity=1 ⇒ distance ≥ 0.108. (d) spatialDigest.js:478 routeReceipts crossStep always uses cellB's entering cost even when the winning crossing was scanned in the reverse orientation — byTerrain attribution (only) can misassign between the two boundary classes, feeding a slightly-off seasonal blend. (e) ENTREPOT_TUNING.DEFAULT_RISK_TOLERANCE (entrepots.js:107) must equal supplyKernel's SUPPLY_RISK_TOLERANCE (both 0.6) for crossings to tally the dispatched routes — coupled by comment only.
  - Why: Individually cosmetic, but (a) undermines the receipt-as-truth discipline the engine markets as constitutional, and (e) is a silent-drift trap for the owner's retune-without-rebuild workflow.
  - Fix shape: (a) band the copy by magnitudeFraction ('a tenth'/'half'/'most'); (b)(c) fix comments; (d) pick costEnter from the winning orientation; (e) import one constant or add an equality pin test.
  - Deferral check: Round-1 spatial-engine-9 nit batch and misc-2(e) checked — none of these five recorded; rumorEventKey 'trade:' consolidation IS recorded there and is deliberately not re-reported.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_

'easing' : canonical.status` — deriveActiveCondition maps a missing/legacy ('active') status to the template default ('worsening' for plague). Traced: tick 1 holds severity; tick 2 reads the written-back 'worsening' and climbs +0.04/tick. tests/domain/activeConditions.test.js:351 pins only ONE tick.

- **[MEDIUM|confirmed] generators-pipeline-1** — stripDerivedConfigKeys is dead code — the derived-config echo guard is never called, and 8+ files attribute it to a nonexistent applyChange flow (`src/store/settlementSlice.js:185`)
  - Evidence: grep across src/: only the definition (settlementSlice.js:185) and tests call it. assembleSettlement.js:112 claims 'applyChange regenerates from _config below, and its legacy fallback strips the derived keys'; no applyChange function exists anywhere in src (grep). SettlementsPanel.jsx:90 legacy fallback `data.config` → migrateConfig (no strip) → updateConfig.
  - Why: The guard exists precisely so emergent stress can't echo back as user-forced input: a legacy save whose stored config is a resolved snapshot re-enters generation with stressTypes/tier/terrainType/_magicTradeOnly/neighborRelationship intact — resolveStress records them as intendedStressTypes and stressConfirmPass then never drops them. Tests assert the strip's behavior, giving false assurance it is wired.
  - Fix shape: Wire stripDerivedConfigKeys into every path that feeds a possibly-resolved config back into generation (SettlementsPanel.onLoad fallback, any _config-absent fallback), or make migrateConfig call it. Sweep the eight 'applyChange' comments to name the real flow (onLoad → updateConfig → generateSettlement). Pin with a legacy-save round-trip test.
  - Deferral check: Round-1 register grep: stripDerivedConfigKeys/applyChange never mentioned; playbook 0.0.2 + DEAD_CODE_DISPOSITION: no entry. Fresh finding.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_

At the assemble call site (line 437) the removed-names return is discarded — no recordTrace, unlike the cascade/faction call sites.

Two of the four roster-pruning mechanisms honor the DM contract, two do not.

- **[HIGH|confirmed] components-map-1** — Autosave dirty-key diverged from AutoSaveChip's content-aware fingerprint — placement moves and annotation edits never autosave while the chip says 'Unsaved changes' (`src/hooks/useMapAutosave.js:23`)
  - Evidence: Hook key: `Object.keys(m.placements||{}).sort().join(',')|${(m.labels||[]).length}|...` (ids+counts only); its header claims 'The key mirrors AutoSaveChip's dirty fingerprint'. AutoSaveChip.jsx:57-72 fingerprint() folds in x/y/cellId/text/color per item, and its docstring calls the count-only key a bug it fixed. flags.js:84 mapAutosave default:true.
  - Why: Drag-moving a placement, renaming a label, or moving a marker/forest flips the chip to permanent 'Unsaved changes' but never fires the autosave; WorldMapToolbar demotes manual Save because 'AutoSaveChip carries reassurance'. Campaign switch (replaceMapState) then discards the edits — the owner's most-bitten write-ghosts-a-path class, live on a default-on flag.
  - Fix shape: Extract AutoSaveChip's fingerprint() to a shared module and make it the hook's dirty key (one source, two consumers); add a parity pin test so the two can never diverge again. Delete the stale 'mirrors AutoSaveChip' comment.
  - Deferral check: Playbook §0.0.2 + round-1 register: no record. Round-1 fixed the CHIP side only (its docstring documents that fix); the hook was left behind — a fresh divergence, not a recorded deferral.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_

Small window, owner's lifecycle-gap class.

- **[HIGH|confirmed] components-dossier-library-1** — WelcomeCreditCard ships the raw Supabase userId as an analytics prop, bypassing the hashUserId privacy floor (`src/components/dossier/WelcomeCreditCard.jsx:98`)
  - Evidence: `Funnel.track(EVENTS.WELCOME_CREDIT_GRANTED, { userId })` (and :113 `WELCOME_CREDIT_SPENT, { userId }`). track()'s 2nd arg is props — docs say 'coarse properties only (no PII)'; only opts.userId is hashed (analytics.js:127-128). Both events are essential-class, so sendToProvider mirrors the RAW id to the third-party provider; enqueueEvent stores it first-party.
  - Why: Direct violation of the privacy-first analytics doctrine (id-free enrichment) and of analytics.js's own hashing floor, on the paid-conversion event pair; docs/analytics-event-dictionary.md declares both events prop-less.
  - Fix shape: Change both calls to `Funnel.track(EVENT, {}, { userId })` (the signupCompleted pattern) so the id rides the hashed opts lane; add a structural test that no Funnel.track call site passes userId/user_id inside props (grep-based source scan, the repo's established prevention idiom).
  - Deferral check: docs/COMPREHENSIVE_REVIEW_2026-07-13.md (no welcome-credit finding), docs/DESIGN_ANALYTICS_V2.md, docs/analytics-event-dictionary.md (declares props '—'), usage-telemetry memory (id-free doctrine). Not recorded anywhere.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[HIGH|confirmed] components-dossier-library-2** — Mutable-docket edit silently inverts a queued IMPOSE_CULT removal into an imposition (editSeed↔buildEvent asymmetry) (`src/components/settlement/eventComposer/editSeed.js:91`)
  - Evidence: `if (p.deityRef != null) fields.deityRef = ...; if (p.deityRef == null) fields.deityMode = 'remove'` — but an IMPOSE_CULT removal's payload is {deityRef: <cultRef>, snapshot: null} (mutateEntities.js:877-890), so deityMode stays 'assign'. reconcileCultImposition returns 'replaced'/'refresh' for a same-ref probe (cultImpositionApply.js:88), so canStageDeityEvent passes; buildEvent's assign branch resolves a snapshot and updateQueuedEvent (campaignSlice.js:773) replaces the queued removal in place.
  - Why: The DM edits a queued 'remove cult X' (even just its description), clicks Apply, and the docket now holds 'impose cult X' — while the UI select shows the placeholder because the seeded deityRef is filtered out of addOptions. Silent intent inversion in the owner-law surface ('each queued commit is editable') shipped with W-COMPOSER-2; editSeed has zero test coverage (no test imports eventToComposerIntent).
  - Fix shape: In editSeed, key the remove detection per type: IMPOSE_CULT remove = `p.snapshot == null` → seed deityMode:'remove' + cultRemoveRef:p.deityRef (add cultRemoveRef to applyComposerIntent's setters map). Then add the missing structural guard: a round-trip pin test asserting buildEvent(applyComposerIntent(eventToComposerIntent(e))) reproduces every queued payload byte-for-byte across all authorable verbs — the whole reverse-map class is untested.
  - Deferral check: docs/DESIGN_EVENT_COMPOSER_V2.md §10 (mutable docket law — no editSeed caveat), W-COMPOSER-2 memory deferrals (budget red/FP-G7 only), editSeed.js in-file notes (only the derived-values non-seeding is recorded as deliberate). Not deferred.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_

### Slice: store-hooks-state (grade A-)

**Coverage:** 52 files / 14,114 LOC in scope (32 store files + 20 hooks). All 52 files fully read line-by-line — no skims, no skips. Load-bearing coupled files outside the slice were additionally read in targeted fashion to confirm cross-module contracts: domain/pendingEdits.js (full), lib/saves.js (supabaseUpdate/List mapping), domain/events/drainQueuedEvents.js (drain refusal path), domain/worldPulse/advanceInterval.js (resume re-derive), components/settlement/EventComposer.jsx and components/WorldMap.jsx (consumer contract excerpts), plus the round-1 register, playbook §0.0.2, and the guard-test files (advanceInFlightMutatorGuard, pulseMutatorsInFlightGuard) for deferral awareness.

- **[HIGH|confirmed] store-hooks-state-1** — Clock-bound queue refusal rides through applyEvent as success — composer resets the form and drops the DM's order silently during in-flight/parked advances (`src/store/settlementSlice.js:1664`)
  - Evidence: applyEvent: `const queued = state.queueSettlementEvent(activeSaveId, event); if (queued) { ...return queued; }` — queueSettlementEvent returns a TRUTHY refusal `{ queued:false, reason:'advance_in_flight'|'advance_paused' }` (campaignSlice.js:694,700). EventComposer.jsx:391-405 only branches on `entry.veto`; a refusal falls through to `resetAfterApply()` and `if (entry && !entry.queued && narrated) setStaleNotice(...)`.
  - Why: During a parked pause (which lasts until the DM resolves majors — potentially a whole session) any event composed on a clock-bound settlement is silently discarded while the UI resets the form and raises the stale-narrative modal: a paying DM's intent drop with success-shaped UX. applyEventBatch likewise counts refusals as successful logEntries.
  - Fix shape: Make applyEvent wrap the refusal in an ok:false ActionResult with userMessage from ADVANCE_ERROR_TEXT; EventComposer branches on `entry.queued === false` (keep the form, toast the reason); applyEventBatch classifies queued:false entries as refusals. Add a composer-during-pause store test beside advancePauseResume.test.js.
  - Deferral check: Not in playbook §0.0.2, round-1 register (store-2 covered the guard itself, whose comment explicitly says 'the UI can toast the reason' — no UI does), or in-file seam notes.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[HIGH|confirmed] store-hooks-state-2** — Outbox ordering guarantee is keyed on exact partial-key sets while call sites write overlapping columns under different kinds — stale-blob resurrection race (`src/store/campaignSliceShared.js:165`)
  - Evidence: kindForPartial: 'writes to the same columns... a version-history-only write and a settlement write coexist (they touch different columns)'. But live kinds all writing the `data` column include 'campaignState+settlement' (applyEvent), 'settlement+timestamp' (applyMapEdit), 'campaignState+settlement+timestamp' (destroy), 'campaignState+settlement+versionHistory' (revert), 'aiData+campaignState+settlement' (applyEvent+narrative). supabaseUpdate has no `timestamp` column at all.
  - Why: Different kinds never share a serialization gate, so a backing-off older op (e.g. a map edit) can land AFTER a newer applyEvent write and revert the settlement column in the cloud; reload then resurrects the undone state — the exact ghost-write class. aiSlice compounds it by writing ai_data via direct savesService.update, bypassing the outbox while applyEvent's op also carries aiData.
  - Fix shape: Key the per-op gate on the mapped COLUMN set (strip non-column keys like timestamp), or serialize all same-saveId ops whose column sets intersect; route aiSlice's ai_data writes through persistSaveUpdate so one lane owns the column. Add an interleaved-kinds outbox test.
  - Deferral check: outbox.js/campaignSliceShared.js headers assert the guarantee (no recorded caveat); not in §0.0.2, round-1 register, or DEAD_CODE_DISPOSITION.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[HIGH|confirmed] store-hooks-state-3** — Residual worldpulse-core-1 gap: rules edits and spatial canonize during a parked pause are silently reverted by resume — no store guard, no UI gate (`src/store/campaignWorldPulseSlice.js:330`)
  - Evidence: updateCampaignSimulationRules guards only `isAdvanceInFlight` (line 334); canonizeCampaignWorldSpatial likewise (line 312). advanceInterval.js:315-318 on resume: `runningCampaign = { ...runningCampaign, worldState: resume.preWorldState, ... }` and applyWorldPulseResultToState then replaces c.worldState wholesale. useRealmInspector.rulesEditBlocked checks only advanceInFlight.
  - Why: A DM who edits simulation rules (or re-canonizes spatially) while an interval is parked for verdicts loses the edit, its rulesetLog receipt, AND its wizard-news line the moment they resume — the exact clobber class worldpulse-core-1 fixed for proposals/party/docket. SimulationRulesDialog has zero paused gating, so the window is fully reachable.
  - Fix shape: Add the getPausedAdvance sync-prefix (typed advance_paused no-op) to updateCampaignSimulationRules, canonizeCampaignWorld, canonizeCampaignWorldSpatial; extend rulesEditBlocked to include the parked-pause state. Extend pulseMutatorsInFlightGuard.test.js with pause-window cases.
  - Deferral check: Round-1 worldpulse-core-1 fix record (COMPREHENSIVE_REVIEW_PROGRAM.md F1) lists 'pause-window mutation guards + panel gating' — rules/spatial-canonize were not in the fixed set and no deferral note exists in-file or in §0.0.2.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[MEDIUM|confirmed] store-hooks-state-4** — resolveRegionalImpact and advanceCampaignRegionalImpacts missed the store-2 advance-in-flight guard their siblings received (`src/store/campaignRegionalSlice.js:564`)
  - Evidence: applyQueuedRegionalImpact opens with the store-2 guard (line 456: `if (...isAdvanceInFlight(campaignId)) return null;`); resolveRegionalImpact (line 564) and advanceCampaignRegionalImpacts (line 427) have none, yet both mutate savedSettlements/regionalGraph across an await that a running advance's Phase-2 wholesale replace clobbers.
  - Why: Resolving an impact mid-advance yields split truth: the graph marks 'resolved' after the awaited save, but the advance's settlementUpdates recommit the pre-resolve settlement — the condition resurrects while 'resolved' blocks re-resolve, permanently. The exact class store-2 fixed elsewhere.
  - Fix shape: Add the isAdvanceInFlight + getPausedAdvance sync-prefix to both actions (typed null no-op, matching applyQueuedRegionalImpact); add cases to advanceInFlightMutatorGuard.test.js.
  - Deferral check: store-2's fix enumerated queueSettlementEvent/cancel/setRegionalImpactStatus/applyQueuedRegionalImpact/undoBridge; resolveRegionalImpact absent from the record and from §0.0.2 — an omission, not a ruling.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[MEDIUM|confirmed] store-hooks-state-5** — Town rename never persists the row `name` column — renamed settlements revert their display name after reload on every row-name surface (`src/store/settlementRenameHelpers.js:114`)
  - Evidence: persist = `{ settlement: cloneJson(nextSettlement), ...(isCanon ? { campaignState..., timestamp } : {}) }` — no `name` key, though supabaseUpdate maps `partial.name` and supabaseList/listMeta return `name: row.name`. Repo-wide grep: no persistSaveUpdate call ever carries `name`. CampaignFolder.jsx:26 reads `match?.name` first.
  - Why: The in-memory entry gets name:trimmed but the cloud row keeps the old name forever; after reload the library list, campaign folders, and the blob-less meta list (settlement:null — no fallback possible) all show the pre-rename name. A §10.4 ghost the persist-gap wave missed.
  - Fix shape: Add `name: trimmed` to the persist partial in renameSettlementImpl (both canon and draft arms); pin with a rename→list round-trip test beside editActionPersist.test.js.
  - Deferral check: Not in the round-1 register (searched renameSettlement/name column), §10.4 fix record (151a8ee3 covered edit-action blob persistence), or in-file notes.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[MEDIUM|confirmed] store-hooks-state-6** — addToCampaign strands the moved settlement's queued intentions in the old campaign, where the drain silently destroys them (`src/store/campaignSlice.js:473`)
  - Evidence: addToCampaign strips the settlement from every other campaign's settlementIds but never touches their worldState.pendingEvents; removeFromCampaign explicitly drops them ('otherwise they'd be silently destroyed at the next tick'). drainQueuedEvents.js:88 `if (!save || !save.settlement) continue;` — no refusal — then the drain clears pendingEvents wholesale.
  - Why: Re-homing a settlement (single or via useLibraryBulkSelect.addToCampaignBulk) leaves its queued orders in campaign A; A's next advance vaporizes them with no refusal news — violating the W-COMPOSER-2 §10 'refused VISIBLY, never silently dropped' law and the mutable-docket promise.
  - Fix shape: In addToCampaign, apply removeFromCampaign's pendingEvents drop (or migrate entries to the target campaign) for every campaign the settlement leaves; additionally make the drain emit a 'not_member' refusal instead of `continue`.
  - Deferral check: removeFromCampaign's own comment proves the class was known and fixed on one path only; no deferral for the add/move path in §0.0.2 or the register.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[MEDIUM|confirmed] store-hooks-state-7** — useMapBridge documents addPlacement as 'the authoritative gate' returning {ok,reason} — but mapSlice.addPlacement has no gate and returns undefined; the bridge path places ungated (`src/hooks/useMapBridge.js:75`)
  - Evidence: 'addPlacement is the authoritative gate (campaign / canon / no-duplicate)... rejected here without mutating' then `if (res && res.ok === false) showToast(...)`. mapSlice.addPlacement (mapSlice.js:284-335) unconditionally writes the placement and returns nothing; WorldMap.jsx:108 passes the raw store action.
  - Why: A settlementPlaced bridge event that bypasses handleDrop's pre-checks (direct FMG placement, re-entrant echo) mutates placements with no campaign/canon/duplicate check; PLACEMENT_REJECT_COPY is dead code and the documented refusal toast can never fire — the backstop the hook promises does not exist.
  - Fix shape: Implement the typed gate in mapSlice.addPlacement (return {ok:false, reason:'no-campaign'|'not-canon'|'duplicate'} using the same checks handleDrop performs) so both entry points share one authoritative gate; the hook's consumer code already expects that shape.
  - Deferral check: Round-1 components-dossier-1 covered the image-mode drop contract, not this bridge-event gate; no deferral note in mapSlice, useMapBridge, or §0.0.2.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] store-hooks-state-8** — useCampaignAutoResume reads store field lastActiveCampaignId that nothing ever writes — the 'resume last-used campaign' behavior is dead (`src/hooks/useCampaignAutoResume.js:45`)
  - Evidence: `const lastActiveCampaignId = useStore(s => s.lastActiveCampaignId);` — repo-wide grep finds zero writers and no slice declares the field; partialize wouldn't persist it anyway. Doc claims 'this reopens the campaign the user last used'.
  - Why: The selector is always undefined, so auto-resume silently always picks activeCampaigns[0] (most-recently-updated) — correct-ish by coincidence but divergent from the documented contract, and a trap for anyone extending resume behavior.
  - Fix shape: Stamp lastActiveCampaignId in setActiveCampaign and persist it (localStorage alongside the campaign cache, keyed by owner), or delete the dead branch and fix the doc to say most-recently-updated.
  - Deferral check: Not in DEAD_CODE_DISPOSITION.md, §0.0.2, or the round-1 register; no in-file seam note.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|plausible] store-hooks-state-9** — initOutbox on App mount re-runs loadMirror, wiping the live op queue and in-flight gates that earlier child effects may already have populated (`src/store/campaignSliceShared.js:347`)
  - Evidence: outbox.js module-load already primes via loadMirror(); App.jsx:190 calls initOutbox() in an effect, and loadMirror() resets `_ops = []; _inflightByKey = new Map()`. Child-component effects (auto-resume catch-up persist, loadCampaigns) run before the parent App effect and can enqueue/attempt ops first.
  - Why: An op inflight at that instant loses its serialization gate and its queue entry while the mirror (which normalized it to 'queued') is replayed by drainReady — a duplicate concurrent write to the same row, breaking the ordering guarantee in a narrow boot window.
  - Fix shape: Make loadMirror merge into a non-empty in-memory queue (or no-op when _ops is non-empty and gates exist), or run initOutbox at store-module init instead of an App effect.
  - Deferral check: No note in outbox.js/initOutbox headers, §0.0.2, or the register; the module-load prime comment suggests the double-load was seen but the reset interaction is unaddressed.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_

FULLY READ (~48 files, ~11,500 LOC): the entire analytics seam (analytics, analyticsEvents, analyticsQueue, analyticsQueueState, analyticsFlush, analyticsProvider, consent, sessionId, session, researchCapture, generationTelemetry, spatialUsage), supabase, auth, gallery, saves, campaignSync, accountImport, importScrub, stripe, checkoutReconcile, creditLedger(head), pendingDossier, dossierClaimStash, dossierRetroClaim, dossierEntitlements, emailLifecycle, emailTemplates, mapThumb, mapBridge, entityRefTokenizer, sanitizeGalleryHtml, computeMapChains, routes, flags, guidance, errorReporter, seo, chronicle, narrativeMutations, anonGenCounter, authIntents, pendingSaveDraft, deviceToken, App.jsx, AppViews.jsx, main.jsx, copy/index, config/tierFacts, generateSettlementPDF, pdfRender.worker, pdfWorkerShim. - **[MEDIUM|confirmed] lib-infra-copy-1** — spatialUsage adoption telemetry is blind to every post-A1 mover layer — naval, epidemic, disinfo/credibility, upswing, resource-dynamics, momentum flags and ledgers are absent from both whitelists, with no registration convention forcing new waves to extend them (`src/lib/spatialUsage.js:69`)

  - Why: The module's stated product purpose is 'which mover layers actually get exercised in real campaigns'. Every wave landed since A1 will report as never-adopted, silently corrupting the exact adoption signal the analytics program was built to answer; docs/analytics-event-taxonomy.md:266 pins the stale list as the contract.
  - Fix shape: Extend TRACKED_FLAGS + MOVER_PRESENCE for the merged waves, regenerate the taxonomy dictionary, and add a walker test that diffs spatialLedgers keys written by domain code against MOVER_PRESENCE coverage (with an explicit exempt list) — the same only-extends pin idiom as the resource-classification EXPECTED table.
  - Deferral check: No record in playbook §0.0.2/§0.6, DESIGN_ANALYTICS_V2.md, the W-UPSWING/W-DISCOVERY memory deferral lists, tests/lib/spatialUsage.test.js, or in-file comments — the whitelists simply predate the waves.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] lib-infra-copy-2** — consent.setConsent docstring promises updatedAt 'defaults to now' but the code stamps cur.updatedAt+1 — consent-change wall-clock provenance is never recorded (`src/lib/consent.js:118`)
  - Evidence: Docstring: "`stampMs` lets callers pass a deterministic timestamp (tests); defaults to now." Code: `updatedAt: typeof stampMs === 'number' ? stampMs : (cur.updatedAt + 1)`. Sole caller PrivacySettings.jsx:65 passes no stampMs, so a fresh opt-out stores updatedAt:1.
  - Why: The v2 opt-out flip leans on updatedAt as explicit-choice provenance; losing the actual timestamp weakens the audit trail for WHEN a user opted out (GDPR-adjacent), and the doc-vs-code drift invites a future maintainer to 'fix' it either way silently.
  - Fix shape: Stamp Date.now() as the default (still >0, tests keep stampMs); or fix the docstring and record the counter choice in-file as deliberate.
  - Deferral check: tests/lib/consent.test.js pins only 'positive updatedAt'; no in-file or playbook record explains the +1 choice.
