### Slice: backend-functions (grade A)

**Coverage:** Scope: 54 files / 29,065 LOC under supabase/functions/. HEAD read: 1f7e20c7 (descendant of expected 91a0c409; only round-2 program bookkeeping between). FULL-READ (~16.5k LOC): all 16 function handlers (stripe-webhook 1496, admin-actions 1284, generate-narrative 2036, account-actions 646, create-checkout 619, auth-recovery 480, send-email 496, generate-chronicle 415, ingest-events 355, og-image 316, verify-single-dossier 267, pricing-resync-cron 253, analytics-export 151, verify-checkout-session 140, create-customer-portal 128, log-client-error 136), all 7 generate-narrative helpers (prompts 1548, entityRefWrapper 239, inventionSignal 117, jsonUtils 67, promptCache 37, refundPolicy 32), and all 5 hand-written _shared modules (pricingResync 988, cors 210, requestMeta 161, referralEmails 156, logError 65) plus both bundle meta.json files. GENERATED bundles (aiGroundingBundle.js 8,748 + analyticsEventsBundle.js 200): banner + header skimmed, freshness CONFIRMED by independently recomputing the gate's sha256 recipe (both match: 4b29d2b507c8ea9b / 012883267b05bebb) — no hand-edit evidence; sources reviewed by the domain slices. SKIMMED: tests/edgeFunctions/aiGroundingBundle.freshness.test.js (read in part), test filenames + supabase/config.toml verify_jwt pins (every function explicitly pinned). SKIPPED: the ~7.2k LOC of *.test.ts / tests/edgeFunctions suites (verification layer; production handlers were the target; running them was forbidden). Deferral ledgers checked: docs/COMPREHENSIVE_REVIEW_2026-07-13.md round-1 register (backend-1..7 + nit batches + Phase-V refutations), docs/COMPREHENSIVE_REVIEW_PROGRAM.md fix status, in-file deferral notes.

- **[MEDIUM|confirmed] backend-functions-1** — Founder clawback throws AFTER the atomic is_founder claim — a transient failure permanently strands a refunded founder with premium tier and the 30-credit bonus (`supabase/functions/stripe-webhook/index.ts:611`)
  - Evidence: After the claim-once flip (`.update({ is_founder: false }).eq('is_founder', true)`), `if (downgradeErr) throw new Error(...)` and `if (authErr) throw ...` — but the file's own failure-posture doc (lines 292-297) says post-claim errors must log-not-throw because "the throw buys no retry, it just hides the partial state". Traced: redelivery finds is_founder already false, claims no row, returns early.
  - Why: A refunded/disputed founder whose handle_premium_downgrade or GoTrue call transiently fails keeps tier=premium and the credit bonus forever: the released event claim makes Stripe redeliver, but the redelivery no-ops on the claim check. Unlike the referral clawback, no row records what remains owed.
  - Fix shape: Match the referral-clawback posture: after the is_founder claim, run downgrade/auth/credit steps each in its own try/catch with logError, or make them idempotent and re-runnable outside the claim gate. Optionally stamp a founder_clawbacks remediation row like the referrals surface.
  - Deferral check: Round-1 register + Phase-V verdicts: no entry for founder clawback posture; the failure-posture rule it violates is the file's own documented contract. Not in playbook §0.0.2 / DEAD_CODE_DISPOSITION.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[MEDIUM|plausible] backend-functions-3** — Shared CORS Vercel suffix rule is spoofable: an attacker-named Vercel project's production alias ends with '-settlement-forge.vercel.app' (`supabase/functions/_shared/cors.ts:134`)
  - Evidence: `if (url.protocol === 'https:' && url.hostname.endsWith(VERCEL_DEPLOY_SUFFIX)) return true;` — the comment asserts "The team scope is the trailing segment and is owned by us — an attacker cannot deploy under it", but Vercel production aliases are `<project>.vercel.app` in a global first-come namespace: project name `evil-settlement-forge` yields `evil-settlement-forge.vercel.app`, which passes the suffix check.
  - Why: The allowlist's fail-closed invariant (documented as spoof-proof) is falsifiable: a hostile origin gets an exact ACAO echo plus Allow-Credentials:true on every edge function. Practical impact is bounded (auth is bearer-token, not cookie), but the module's central security claim is wrong as written.
  - Fix shape: Tighten to the deploy-URL shape the comment actually assumes: require the full `<project>-<hash>-settlement-forge.vercel.app` pattern (e.g. /^[a-z0-9-]+-[a-z0-9]{9}-settlement-forge\.vercel\.app$/) or enumerate via ALLOWED_ORIGINS; add a spoof-shaped negative case to cors.test.ts.
  - Deferral check: No round-1 entry (the Vercel rule postdates round 1); not in playbook §0.0.2 or any in-file deferral note — the comment claims safety rather than deferring it.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] backend-functions-2** — verify-checkout-session still bypasses the shared fail-closed CORS module (round-1 backend-5, still open — not a regression) (`supabase/functions/verify-checkout-session/index.ts:48`)
  - Evidence: `'Access-Control-Allow-Origin': accepted ? (origin || '*') : allowed[0]` with an inline allowlist missing the Cloudflare Pages / Vercel preview rules — the exact drift _shared/cors.ts exists to eliminate; every sibling imports sharedCorsHeaders.
  - Why: Emits ACAO '*' on missing-Origin requests (posture the shared module forbids) and breaks post-checkout purchase confirmation from Cloudflare/Vercel preview deploys, the same class of bug that motivated the shared module.
  - Fix shape: Replace corsHeaders() with `sharedCorsHeaders(req, { methods: 'POST, OPTIONS' })` — a five-line diff identical to what create-checkout did; add it to cors.test.ts's adopters list.
  - Deferral check: docs/COMPREHENSIVE_REVIEW_2026-07-13.md line 1380: [backend-5] LOW/confirmed — recorded, verified NOT yet fixed at HEAD (fix program lists backend-1/-3 as done, not -5). Reported at the register's own severity.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] backend-functions-4** — Pre-parse body-byte caps are inconsistently applied: five anon-reachable functions buffer unbounded request bodies (`supabase/functions/create-checkout/index.ts:312`)
  - Evidence: `const { product, checkoutToken, ... } = await req.json();` runs before the rate limiter and the MAX_DOSSIER_BYTES check (which re-serializes post-parse); auth-recovery:261, send-email:359, log-client-error:81 parse unbounded anon bodies; stripe-webhook:909 `await req.text()` buffers before signature rejection. ingest-events/generate-narrative/generate-chronicle all cap bytes pre-parse.
  - Why: The codebase established the byte-cap discipline (and fixed the UTF-16 variant with regression tests) but never swept it across the anon surface; a single oversized POST forces full buffering+parse per request, bounded only by platform limits.
  - Fix shape: One _shared/readBodyCapped.ts helper (text + byte cap + parse) adopted by all five; caps per function (create-checkout ~600KB to fit the 512KB stash, others 64KB). Mirrors the existing generate-chronicle pattern.
  - Deferral check: Round-1 register: body caps flagged and fixed only for generate-narrative/chronicle/ingest-events; no recorded decision to exempt the rest. No in-file deferral notes at the parse sites.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] backend-functions-5** — checkout.session.async_payment_failed is unhandled — a reserved redeem-code seat leaks permanently on a deferred payment that fails (`supabase/functions/stripe-webhook/index.ts:1176`)
  - Evidence: The switch handles completed/async_payment_succeeded/expired but `grep async_payment_failed` finds nothing in supabase/functions. Traced: a session that completes with payment_status='unpaid' defers fulfillment; if the async payment then fails, `expired` never fires for a completed session, so revert_redemption never runs.
  - Why: The bound reservation stays 'reserved' forever: that user's once-per-user row is burned and the code's uses_count stays consumed — deferred money leaked with no operator signal. Rare today (card-only checkout), but the redeem lifecycle claims closure.
  - Fix shape: Add `case 'checkout.session.async_payment_failed':` calling revert_redemption(session.id) — identical body to the expired case; claim-once semantics make it race-safe with a later success.
  - Deferral check: No round-1 entry, no in-file seam note (the async_payment_succeeded comment discusses the success leg only); not in playbook ledgers.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] backend-functions-6** — ingest-events actor first-touch races remain: concurrent device/user link inserts can mint divergent actor ids (round-1 backend-6, still open) (`supabase/functions/ingest-events/index.ts:106`)
  - Evidence: `await admin.from('analytics_device_links').insert({ device_key: deviceKey, actor_id: actor });` — insert error unchecked, freshly-minted actor returned regardless; resolveUserActor's upsert with ignoreDuplicates:true (line 121) returns its own candidate even when a concurrent writer's row won.
  - Why: Two concurrent first-touch envelopes attribute events to an actor id that never became the durable link — a permanent funnel-stitching blemish in the analytics corpus, invisible at write time.
  - Fix shape: Upsert with onConflict:'device_key' then re-select the winning actor_id (same for the identity link) — one round-trip more on the cold path only.
  - Deferral check: docs/COMPREHENSIVE_REVIEW_2026-07-13.md line 1387: [backend-6] LOW/probable — recorded, verified still present at HEAD; carried at the register's severity.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] backend-functions-7** — ingest-events swallows insert failures into a 202 and drops event/edit/pulse overflow uncounted — asymmetric with the counted snapshot cap (`supabase/functions/ingest-events/index.ts:246`)
  - Evidence: `const { error } = await admin.from('analytics_events').upsert(...); if (!error) accepted.events = ...` — on error the response is still 202 with no rejected entry and no log; `body.events.slice(0, 50)` (line 231), edits slice(0,20), pulseEffects slice(0,60) silently discard overflow while snapshots overflow is pushed to `rejected` as 'snapshot_cap_exceeded'.
  - Why: A DB-side failure or an over-batching client loses telemetry invisibly — the exact silent-loss class the SERVER_SNAPSHOT_CAP fix (lib-infra-3) was written to eliminate, still open on the other three planes.
  - Fix shape: Log insert errors (logError) and count overflow rows into `rejected` with 'event_cap_exceeded'-style reasons, mirroring the snapshot treatment; optionally surface accepted<sent to the client queue for re-spill.
  - Deferral check: Round-1 fixed only the snapshot plane (lib-infra-3); no recorded decision covering events/edits/pulse overflow or insert-error swallowing.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] backend-functions-8** — invoice.paid with no matching profile throws forever — a manually-created Stripe customer's invoice retry-loops through the webhook (round-1 nit, still open) (`supabase/functions/stripe-webhook/index.ts:218`)
  - Evidence: `throw new Error(`Monthly allowance invoice ${invoice.id} has no matching profile`)` — the throw releases the event claim, Stripe redelivers on its full backoff schedule, and every delivery re-throws until Stripe abandons (~3 days of noise per invoice).
  - Why: Operator-created or migrated Stripe customers with no profiles row turn into multi-day webhook-failure noise that masks real failures in the dashboard; nothing is fulfillable, so the retries buy nothing.
  - Fix shape: Ack unmatched non-subscription-linked invoices with a structured logError breadcrumb instead of throwing, or throw only when a customerId exists but the email fallback also matched nothing after a bounded retry count.
  - Deferral check: Recorded in the round-1 nit batch (register line ~1396, item 2); verified still present at HEAD — carried forward at low, matching the register.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] backend-functions-9** — admin get_stats selects every profiles row unbounded — silently wrong stats past PostgREST's max-rows and a growing full-table read (`supabase/functions/admin-actions/index.ts:681`)
  - Evidence: `const { data: profiles } = await adminClient.from('profiles').select('tier, role, credits');` then JS-side count/reduce over the returned rows — no .limit, no aggregate; PostgREST caps the response (default 1000 rows), after which total/premiumCount/totalCredits undercount without any error.
  - Why: The admin dashboard's headline numbers silently freeze at the row cap as the user base grows — a wrong-number bug that looks like a plateau, on the surface an operator trusts for business decisions.
  - Fix shape: Replace with one SECURITY DEFINER admin_stats() RPC doing count(*)/sum(credits) grouped by tier/role — same pattern as the existing report_* functions this file already dispatches to.
  - Deferral check: Not in the round-1 register (admin-actions entries covered PII redaction and role gates), no in-file note; checked playbook §0.6 rulings — absent.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] backend-functions-10** — create-checkout ignores the profiles read error from .single() — a profile-less authed user mints a fresh orphan Stripe customer on every checkout (`supabase/functions/create-checkout/index.ts:460`)
  - Evidence: `const { data: profile } = await admin.from('profiles').select('stripe_customer_id').eq('id', user.id).single();` — .single() errors on zero rows, the error is never destructured, profile is undefined, so a new Stripe customer is created and the follow-up `.update(...).eq('id', user.id)` writes zero rows silently.
  - Why: Any user whose profiles row is missing (failed trigger, migration gap) accumulates one orphan Stripe customer per checkout and never gets a durable stripe_customer_id binding — the webhook's email-fallback then carries the whole matching burden for money events.
  - Fix shape: Use .maybeSingle(), check the error, and upsert the profiles binding (or fail loud into the generic 400) — mirrors create-customer-portal's identity-match care on the same read.
  - Deferral check: No round-1 entry or in-file note; the sibling create-customer-portal read at line 72 already uses .maybeSingle(), confirming the intended idiom.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_

### Slice: backend-migrations (grade A-)

**Coverage:** Scope: supabase/migrations/*.sql (134 files, 25,065 LOC), supabase/config.toml (506 LOC), supabase/applied-head.json, supabase/seed_local.sql, supabase/tests/profile_security.sql; no supabase/rollback/ dir exists. FULL-READ (load-bearing security/posture): 038_analytics_rollups, 062_close_authz_gaps, 094_definer_search_path_pg_temp, 111_pin_definer_search_path, 128/129/130 (latent-pantheon + note denylists), 131_repin_definer_search_path (602 LOC), 132/133/134 (analytics v2 + intent atlas), 124_consent_research_optout, config.toml verify_jwt block, tests/lint/migrationSearchPathPin.test.js + its baseline, scripts/check-migration-head.mjs, tests/docs/migrationAppliedHead.test.js. CENSUS-SCANNED all 134 statement-level (python parse): numbering contiguity/duplicates, per-table `enable row level security` + policy counts (51 tables), per-function grant/revoke posture, and search_path pins (replicated the ratchet walker AND traced 094's live-proconfig coverage). SKIMMED (read grant/revoke/RLS lines but not every body line): the mid-chain gallery/billing/admin migrations (008/011/019/024/053/055/066/107/108/114 etc.) — bodies sampled where a function surfaced in the posture census. Did NOT run any gate (read-only discipline; parallel merge session shares the machine).

- **[LOW|confirmed] backend-migrations-1** — Privilege-mutation RPC service_update_profile_metadata retains the default PUBLIC execute grant (no revoke-from-public) (`supabase/migrations/017_fix_credit_auth_integrity.sql:366`)
  - Evidence: Statement-level census across all 134 migrations: the ONLY grant/revoke touching this function is `grant execute on function public.service_update_profile_metadata(uuid, uuid, jsonb, text) to service_role;` (017:366). No migration (incl. 062 'close_authz_gaps', which only drops a policy + enables RLS on 2 tables) ever REVOKEs the inherited PUBLIC grant. Its sibling service_set_credits got exactly this fix in 113.
  - Why: This RPC sets role/tier/is_founder — a privilege-escalation-shaped surface, and 062 GAP-2 explicitly designates it one of the trusted audited write paths after removing the direct profiles-UPDATE bypass. It retains the CREATE-time default PUBLIC execute, so it is callable by any anon/authenticated caller over PostgREST. Not a live hole: the body's first statement `perform public._assert_service_admin_actor(actor_user)` raises unless the JWT role is service_role AND the actor is developer/admin (fail-closed) — the same defense-in-depth gap 113 named for service_set_credits.
  - Fix shape: A grant-only migration mirroring 113: REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.service_update_profile_metadata(uuid,uuid,jsonb,text) FROM public, anon, authenticated; then re-GRANT EXECUTE to service_role. Better: add a structural ratchet (like migrationSearchPathPin) asserting every SECURITY DEFINER money/privilege RPC is revoked from PUBLIC — the absence of such a guard is why this slipped when 113 fixed only one sibling.
  - Deferral check: Checked round-1 backend-3 (search_path, unrelated) and backend-1 (create-checkout rate limit); the 113 migration comment names service_adjust_credits + get_credit_balance as the locked siblings but NOT service_update_profile_metadata; no deferral ledger (playbook 0.0.2/0.6/0.8, DEAD_CODE_DISPOSITION) records this grant gap.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] backend-migrations-2** — search_path ratchet baseline can mask a LIVE pin regression when a baselined pre-094 function is recreated with a bare header (`tests/lint/migrationSearchPathPin.test.js:122`)
  - Evidence: The walker reads search_path from each function's net-current `create or replace` HEADER and is 'blind to ALTER' (its own comment, lines 28-31). 094 pinned the 74 pre-094 definers via an ALTER DO-block; their net-current headers stay bare `set search_path = public` (frozen in the 76-entry baseline). The 'adds no NEW' check only flags names absent from the baseline; the 'no stale' check only flags baselined names that became non-bare.
  - Why: A future migration that recreates any baselined pre-094 function (e.g. get_credit_balance/system_grant_credits) with the historical bare `set search_path = public` header resets its LIVE proconfig to bare public (create-or-replace overwrites proconfig; 094's DO-block never re-runs), reinstating the CVE-2018-1058 pg_temp-first posture — while the ratchet stays green (the name is still bare AND still baselined: neither 'added' nor 'stale'). The guard catches new bare definers and re-pins, but not regressions of already-baselined ones.
  - Fix shape: Have the ratchet resolve the EFFECTIVE pin the way the DB will (treat a baselined function as pinned only if its net-current recreate is <=094 OR its header pins pg_temp), or add a rule that any migration recreating a baselined function must pin `public, pg_temp` in the new header (131's pattern). Low-risk class on Supabase per 094's own note, but the structural-prevention intent is currently defeatable.
  - Deferral check: Checked round-1 backend-3 (resolved by 131 + this ratchet) and the ratchet's own header comments — they acknowledge ALTER-blindness for the 103 service_* baseline entries but do NOT acknowledge the recreate-regression direction; no deferral ledger records it.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] backend-migrations-3** — Applied-head deploy gap widened to 117-vs-134; the pending set now includes security/privacy migrations (128/129/130 pantheon+note, 131 search_path repin) (`supabase/applied-head.json:2`)
  - Evidence: appliedHead=117, repo head=134. classifyAppliedHead returns 'pending' listing 118..134 — which includes 118 (double-charge fix), 119 (idempotency), 121 (_regenSeed strip), 123 (money hardening), 128/129/130 (latent-pantheon + note denylists), and 131 (the search_path repin). check-migration-head warns, never exit 1; the test suite deliberately allows 'pending'.
  - Why: Constitution point 3 (free/anon never see latent deity names/DM truth) and the search_path repin are UNENFORCED in production at applied-head 117 — their SQL is inert until `supabase db push`. The gap is the documented-normal commit→deploy window (round-1 backend-2 REFUTED), but it has widened and now carries the pantheon-leak strip and the money/search_path hardening, so the owner-gated launch push is security-load-bearing, not cosmetic.
  - Fix shape: No code change. At owner-gated launch: run `supabase db push` (applies 118-134 atomically), confirm currency via the SUPABASE_MIGRATION_HEAD deploy probe (fail-closed), then bump appliedHead to 134 in the same PR — exactly the procedure applied-head.json + DEPLOY.md document. Optionally make validate:migration-head FAIL (not warn) when a privacy/money migration is pending.
  - Deferral check: Round-1 backend-2 (REFUTED as documented pre-deploy state, docs/COMPREHENSIVE_REVIEW_2026-07-13.md:2905) + applied-head.json note + DEPLOY.md; recorded here only because the gap widened and now includes the pantheon/search_path security migrations — not re-raised as a bug.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] backend-migrations-4** — Down-migration @rollback annotations are thin on the newest table-creating and hardening migrations (`supabase/migrations/122_dossier_purchases.sql:1`)
  - Evidence: No supabase/rollback/ directory exists; the down-migration convention is inline @rollback annotations present on only 22 of 134 files. The recent NEW-TABLE migrations 122 (dossier_purchases), 125 (user_action_rate_limits), 126 (email_preferences) and the money/privacy hardening 123/127/128/130/131 carry no @rollback line, while nearby peers (113, 129) do.
  - Why: The slice's own convention (e.g. 113/129 document a hand rollback for security tightenings) sets an expectation that reversibility is annotated. New CREATE TABLE migrations have an implicit destructive down (`drop table`), and the hardening migrations have a 'roll back only to unblock a broken deploy, then re-apply' shape — leaving them unannotated makes the reversal path tribal knowledge exactly where a botched launch push would need it. Purely a doc-coverage gap, not a runtime defect.
  - Fix shape: Add a one-line @rollback annotation to the recent table-creating (122/125/126) and hardening (123/127/128/130/131) migrations stating the reversal (drop table / recreate-prior-body), matching the 113/129 precedent; optionally a lint asserting every migration carries an @rollback note.

  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_

- **[MEDIUM|confirmed] tests-estate-1** — layerBoundaries cycle baseline can silently miss a NEW cycle that threads through an already-finished node — enforcement depends on lexicographic DFS order (`tests/architecture/layerBoundaries.test.js:140`)
  - Evidence: `if (s === 1) cycles.add(canonical(stack.slice(stack.indexOf(next)))); else if (s === 0) dfs(next);` — a neighbor in state 2 (done) is ignored. Traced: with allowed cycle helpers>priorityHelpers, adding helpers→newMod→priorityHelpers is recorded ONLY if 'newMod' sorts before 'priorityHelpers'; otherwise priorityHelpers finishes first, newMod→priorityHelpers is a cross edge, and the new 3-cycle is never enumerated — gate stays green.
  - Why: This is the shrink-only architecture ratchet ('adding one → this fails'). The hole clusters exactly around the two ALLOWED cycles' members — helpers.js/priorityHelpers.js are hub generator modules where a new cycle is most likely to form — and whether it is caught depends on file naming, not structure.
  - Fix shape: Replace back-edge enumeration with SCC comparison (Tarjan, ~30 lines): assert every strongly-connected component is a singleton OR exactly equals an allowed cycle's member set. SCCs are traversal-order-invariant, so the baseline becomes airtight instead of name-dependent. Also delete the dead `existsSync(base) ? null : null` at :112.
  - Deferral check: grep of round-1 register + playbook §0.0.2 + DEAD_CODE_DISPOSITION for layerBoundaries/cycle: the gate is referenced as load-bearing (analyticsQueueState cycle-break kept 'ratchet unchanged at 2') but the enumeration hole is nowhere recorded

- **[HIGH|confirmed] determinism-constitution-1** — upswingKernel freshSettlement reads the stale pre-clone updates array — same-tick cross-arc writes clobber each other and can re-arm a completed reconstruction (`src/domain/worldPulse/upswingKernel.js:358`)
  - Evidence: `const freshSettlement = (id) => { const ui = updateIndex.get(String(id)); if (ui !== undefined) return updates[ui]?.settlement; ... }` — reads the ORIGINAL `updates` array, while all writes go to `nextUpdates` after ensureCloned(). The sibling settlementLifecycleKernel.js:471 correctly reads `nextUpdates[ui]?.settlement`.
  - Why: The B2 boom/bust pass (line 570, 594) and B3 flourishing pass (line 664, 677, 698) re-fetch via freshSettlement and then REPLACE nextUpdates[ui].settlement wholesale. If the same settlement was written by an earlier pass the same tick (recon condition mint, recon completion: reconstructedAt stamps + institution upgrade + history beat + skim), those writes are silently lost. Losing the reconstructedAt stamps lets recentUnreconstructedCalamity re-arm the SAME calamity within its 3-year window — a repeated legitimacy dividend/upgrade from one source, violating the kernel's own 'conservation absolute' header. Deterministic, but incoherent composition in a constitutional kernel.
  - Fix shape: Change freshSettlement to read nextUpdates (the lifecycle-kernel idiom) — one-word fix; add a same-tick composition pin (recon-completes + boom-busts same tick; boom-mints + flourish-mints same tick) proving no write is lost. Consider extracting the shared freshSettlement/ensureCloned helper so the two kernels cannot diverge again.
  - Deferral check: PHASE55 playbook §0.0.2 + W-UPSWING/W-DISCOVERY ledger rows, w-upswing-shipped memory deferrals (bust seam / FORCE_CALAMITY / calamityLedger — none cover this), COMPREHENSIVE_REVIEW_2026-07-13 upswing mentions, in-file seam notes, upswingKernel.test.js (no same-tick composition test, no stale/deferred note)
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_
- **[LOW|confirmed] determinism-constitution-2** — upswingKernel header and JSDoc claim seeded forks and an rng param the code never uses (`src/domain/worldPulse/upswingKernel.js:340`)
  - Evidence: Header line 26: 'Deterministic: seeded forks on `upswing:${id}:${tick}`'; JSDoc line 335 declares `@param [args.rng]` with a fork shape; the destructure `advanceUpswing({ snapshot, worldState, settlementUpdates, graph, tick, now })` omits rng and the kernel performs zero draws (pulseKernel:2242 still passes `rng`).
  - Why: Doc-vs-code drift in a constitutional kernel's determinism contract: a future maintainer adding a draw may assume a fork discipline that does not exist, or a reviewer may hunt for forks that were never made. Zero-draw is MORE deterministic, so no Law-1 breach — purely a contract-legibility defect.
  - Fix shape: Either delete the fork claim + rng param (and the pulseKernel pass-through), or if B-arc draws are planned, wire the documented keyed fork now. One-line doc fix otherwise.
  - Deferral check: In-file seam notes (B2/B3 seams documented, none mention rng); W-UPSWING playbook row; no deferral covers the drift
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_

- **[LOW|confirmed] determinism-constitution-4** — resourceDynamicsKernel stamps lastDiscoveryTick/lastRemovalTick at candidate EMISSION, not application — a failed probability roll or DM dismissal still spends the 52-tick cooldown, and the field names overstate what happened (`src/domain/worldPulse/resourceDynamicsKernel.js:303`)
  - Evidence: `nextLastDiscoveryTick = tick;` fires when the candidate is pushed; the candidate then still faces `probability: clamp01(T.DISCOVERY_EMIT_P + nextAcc * 0.22)` (≈0.16–0.38) in rollCandidates and possible DM dismissal. Tuning comment says 'DISCOVERY_COOLDOWN: 52, // ticks (weeks) between discoveries at one settlement' — in practice it is between ATTEMPTS.
  - Why: A settlement whose armed discovery rolls low goes silent for a game-year with the accumulator held above the floor and no receipt; a worked-out vein whose removal candidate fails waits another year despite 'the vein is done'. Deterministic and matching the moral-founding precedent's mechanism (which honestly names its stamp lastCandidateTick), so likely deliberate cadence — but the naming + tuning comments misdescribe the semantics, and unlike upswing/lifecycle there is no deferral-visible receipt for the cooldown-suppressed year.
  - Fix shape: Rename to lastCandidateTick / fix the tuning comments (the precedent's honest naming), or stamp on application via the apply-path writer if between-discoveries semantics is truly wanted; optionally emit a cooldown-suppressed receipt for deferral visibility.
  - Deferral check: In-file notes cite 'the moral-founding cadence idiom' (mechanism deliberate; naming drift is not covered); DESIGN_RESOURCE_DYNAMICS deferral section not contradicted; W-DISCOVERY memory hazards list does not cover it
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V_

- **[MEDIUM|confirmed] state-lifecycle-1** — Town rename never reaches the save row's `name` — library list and name-keyed partner lookup keep the old name on every path (`src/store/settlementSlice.js:523`)
  - Evidence: Queue dispatch: `set(s => { if (s.settlement) { s.settlement.name = edit.payload?.newName; ... } }); if (renamed) get().persistActiveSaveEdit?.();` — persistActiveSaveEdit writes only {settlement, campaignState}. The dormant helper (settlementRenameHelpers.js:114) sets entry `name: trimmed` in memory but its persist partial also omits `name`, and supabaseUpdate maps `partial.name` only when present.
  - Why: The only live town-rename path (DossierHeaderRow → queueEdit('rename-settlement') → commitPendingEdits) updates the blob but never the save envelope: SettlementCard renders `{s.name}` (stale in-session AND after reload), and supabaseListActiveByName resolves neighbour back-link partners by the row `name` column — a rename breaks future partner resolution. Exactly the owner's survives-one-path-ghosts-another class; the §10.4 pin (editActionPersist.test.js:159) asserts only the blob name, so the gap is untested.
  - Fix shape: Route the queue dispatch through renameSettlementImpl (one writer), add `name: trimmed` to its in-memory entry update AND its persistSaveUpdate partial; extend the §10.4 pin to assert persistedEntry(store).name and the cloud partial carries `name`.
  - Deferral check: Round-1 register grep (rename-settlement/renameSettlement/name column) — only the §10.4 blob-ghost fix recorded (shipped); playbook §0.0.2 KNOWN OPEN FINDINGS — no match; DESIGN_SETTLEMENT_MAP §10.4 covers blob persist only.
  - Verdict: _pending Phase V round 2_
- **[MEDIUM|confirmed] state-lifecycle-2** — Deleting a saved settlement leaves campaign residue: settlementIds, mapState placements, and queued pendingEvents are never pruned (`src/components/SettlementsPanel.jsx:405`)
  - Evidence: deleteConfirmed cleans neighbourNetwork/ISR in sibling saves then `persistBatch(updated, modifiedIds, { deletes: [id] })` — no campaign touch. removeFromCampaign (campaignSlice.js:496-513) deliberately prunes membership + queued intentions ('otherwise they'd be silently destroyed at the next tick'), but no delete path calls it; AccountPage bulk delete likewise only removeSavedSettlement.

The campaign row also retains per-settlement worldState ledgers keyed by the dead id indefinitely.
  - Fix shape: In the delete/bulk-delete flow (and destroySavedSettlement if desired), call removeFromCampaign for every campaign holding the id (it already handles queue pruning + persist); add a pin: delete a member save → campaign.settlementIds and pendingEvents no longer reference it.

The caller then clears the queue wholesale: `return { worldState: { ...ws, pendingEvents: [] } ... }` (campaignPulseHelpers.js:258).

- **[LOW|plausible] state-lifecycle-6** — Persistence outbox mirror is not owner-scoped — cross-account boot replay prunes the prior user's parked writes as success (`src/store/outbox.js:38`)

- **[LOW|confirmed] state-lifecycle-8** — undoLastEvent leaves the event-keyed narrative snapshot orphaned in aiData (`src/store/settlementSlice.js:1952`)
  - Evidence: applyEvent archives the pre-event narrative into save.aiData.eventNarrativeSnapshots keyed by event id (line 1786); undoLastEvent scrubs impairments, conditions, authored records, and the log entry, but never touches aiData — the snapshot keyed by the now-nonexistent event survives.
  - Why: Bounded (FIFO cap 10) and cosmetic, but the archive's contract is 'the narrative valid BEFORE this event'; after undo the key refers to an event no longer in any timeline, and a later re-apply of a same-typed event mints a different id, so the orphan never dedupes away.

  - Evidence: `pendingEvents: cloneArray(raw?.pendingEvents).slice(-MAX_PENDING)` (cap 400); queueSettlementEvent appends without checking the bound, so the 401st enqueue causes the oldest intention to vanish on the next ensure with no refusal or digest line.

  - Fix shape: Route the two aiData writes through persistSaveUpdate (kind 'aiData' dedupes/supersedes naturally); keep the aiError message as the immediate surface. Verify the outbox payload cap tolerates large narratives (MAX_QUEUED prunes oldest, blobs stored once per key).

- **[HIGH|confirmed] code-quality-architecture-1** — The domain size ratchet's grandfather mechanism is 'max-lines: off' — unbounded growth, no staleness lock; pulseKernel grew +28% effective in the two days since F7 and the stressors.js win was never locked (`eslint.config.js:518`)
  - Evidence: "'max-lines': 'off', // grandfathered — shrink-only burn-down worklist". Measured at F7 (9b9f7a28) vs HEAD: pulseKernel 1092→1402 eff, applyWorldPulse 769→948 (added to the list 6→7 at the golden merge), warDeployment 1077→1124, stressors 860→477 — now BELOW the 800 ceiling yet its override remains; inline size comments all stale.
  - Why: The one guard covering 'the highest-judgment code in the tree' (its own words) cannot see growth in exactly the files growing fastest. Every JSON baseline in the house fails on stale entries; this override list has no equivalent, so shrink-only is asserted, not enforced.
